Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n72\t`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n73\t\n74\t> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n75\t> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n76\t> reliability or evidence.\n77\t\n78\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n79\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n80\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n81\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n82\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n83\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n84\t- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n85\t- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n86\t\n87\t## Code review rules\n88\t\n89\t- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n90\t- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n91\t- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n92\t- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n93\t- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n94\t- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n95\t- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n\n## Parallel coding agents\n\nSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\nany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n\n1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n   lands in another agent's tree.\n2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n\nSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n\n## Coding conventions\n\n- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- behavior works end-to-end;\n- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n- trace and audit are visible;\n- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n\n## The DX Simplicity Gate\n\nBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\nanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n\n> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n> reliability or evidence.\n\n- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more manual. \"The agent now has one more thing to run\" fails the first bullet.\n\n## Code review rules\n\n- Flag any API/MCP handler that executes SQL directly.\n- Flag any mutation without actor context and audit event.\n- Flag AI-generated business decisions that are not encoded as explicit policy or approval.\n- Flag money represented as floating-point currency amounts.\n- Flag domain-specific business behavior added to `packages/core` without the ADR-018 justification.\n- Flag a JTBD row promoted without linked evidence, or a document claiming a capability the tests do not prove.\n- Flag a new agent-facing command, tool or contract that does not clear the DX Simplicity Gate above.\n","numLines":96,"startLine":1,"totalLines":96}}}

Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n\nIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n\n**Background, where they exist:** `docs/strategy/OBJECTIVE_DRIVEN_AGENT_EXPERIENCE.md`, `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, `docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`, with the worked example in `docs/strategy/OBJECTIVE_DRIVEN_FUNNEL_EXAMPLE.md`. They are the deeper source for the steps below, not a prerequisite for them — the steps stand on their own.\n\nThis workflow is usable **today**, including where capabilities are missing — because reporting a gap honestly is part of the deliverable, not a failure of it.\n\n## 1. Restate the goal before touching anything\n\n1. Restate the business outcome in one sentence and confirm it.\n2. Name the **primary metric**. A goal with no metric cannot be verified in step 8 — ask for one rather than inventing it.\n3. Name the scope, the constraints and the sensitive-action boundaries.\n\n## 2. Discover what actually exists\n\nRead, do not assume — and read whichever of these the project actually has: `AGENTS.md` · `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` · `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` · the JTBD matrix · `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` · the composition file `packages/domains/generated/index.js` · each package's README. In a project built from this framework most of those are absent; the report below is then the whole of your discovery, and a document you cannot open is never a document you may summarise.\n\n**Start here — the command from *Orient yourself first*, re-read now with the goal in mind:**\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- project doctor --json # FIRST: is this project even coherent? (composition,\n                                     # module state, stale plans, skill drift, broken links,\n                                     # tracked artifacts). ~150 ms. Read `problems` and stop\n                                     # if the composition is invalid — everything after it is\n                                     # guesswork against a project that does not load.\nnpm run crm -- app inspect --json    # installed packages, the resolved capability\n                                     # graph, records and their revisions, actions,\n                                     # policies, providers, problems, limitations\n```\n\nRead it in this order, and do not skip a step:\n\n1. `valid` — is the composition sound at all?\n2. `problems[]` — every missing capability, version mismatch, collision and cycle, each with a code. Fix or report these before planning anything on top of them.\n3. `limitations[]` — what the report **cannot** know. Each has a machine-readable code; treat every one as a hard boundary on what you may claim.\n4. `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]` — what you may build on.\n\nExit codes are the contract: `0` valid · `1` problems (the full report is still printed) · `2` the project could not be read.\n\nThen, where you need per-package or runtime detail:\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- package inspect <package-dir>    # one package in isolation\n```\n\n```text\nGET /api/schema     # the same picture from a running server\n```\n\nGuide, where the project carries it: `docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`. The report's own `limitations[]` state the same boundaries without it.\n\n**What `app inspect` does not tell you, and you must not infer:**\n\n- whether any **database** has applied a migration, holds data, or holds *good* data — it reads source only;\n- whether a **provider** is configured, authenticated or reachable. A provider entry means a definition was composed in source, nothing more. Never infer credentials;\n- any **runtime authorization**. There is no auth, tenancy or RBAC in this framework, so no role is enforced anywhere;\n- **JTBD or quality-gate status.** `evidence.status` is `not_aggregated` and carries paths, not claims. Read the documents yourself.\n\nVerify the reliability of source **data** separately: `app inspect` says a record exists, never that its rows are complete, deduplicated or correct. Count the nulls yourself.\n\nThe JTBD matrix is the repository's own honest statement of what is supported. Treat a row marked `not supported` as authoritative over your intuition.\n\n**Where the report will look thinner than the repository.** Three domains — Lead Intelligence, Commercial Operations, and Signature & Order — predate the domain package seam and live in `packages/core/src/`. Their actions and record revisions are reported; there is **no package or capability of theirs to cite**, and that absence means \"built before the seam\", not \"missing\". `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` records exactly what each domain has. Do not plan an extraction to fix it: extraction is sequenced work with its own gate, and a goal-driven build is not the place for it.\n\n**How you reach these surfaces.** A shell command, an exit code and JSON — that is the whole requirement (`docs/AGENT_HARNESS_COMPATIBILITY.md`). The MCP server this repository ships (`docs/MCP.md`) predates AX1 and AX2 and exposes a sample domain rather than these contracts; prefer `app inspect`. Mirroring the stable contracts as MCP reads is **DX13, not built**, and `docs/architecture/AGENT_TOOL_SURFACE.md` is the policy it would be built against — read it before proposing any new tool, and never claim a tool listed there exists.\n\n## 3. Assess the gap in writing\n\nProduce: capability coverage, package reuse, missing capabilities, **data-quality gaps**, provider gaps, hard dependencies, approval requirements and risks. Count the nulls before quoting a rate — a metric computed over mostly-missing data is misleading, and saying so is your job.\n\n## 4. Design: reuse, then extend, never patch the kernel\n\n1. **Prefer an existing official or custom package.** Duplicating a domain that already exists is the most common failure here.\n2. **Create a custom package** when nothing provides what you need — `definePackage`, declared resources, declared capability dependencies, versioned policies, function-free metadata (`docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`).\n3. **Never patch the kernel.** If you need something the runtime lacks, that is a missing *generic* runtime capability: raise it as an ADR discussion, do not reach into `packages/core/src`.\n4. A record that must gain a field or a status **evolves** — bump its manifest `revision` (ADR-019, `docs/MODULE_EVOLUTION.md`). Do not model a second parallel record to work around the schema.\n5. A decision that varies by business is a **versioned, fingerprinted policy**, not a hardcoded branch.\n\n## 5. Produce a Solution Plan before writing code\n\nThe plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings — `solutionPlanContract: 1`. The validator below is the contract; `docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md` and the canonical example `examples/solution-plans/lead-to-won.plan.json` are background where the project carries them.\n\n```bash\nnpm run crm -- solution validate <plan.json>   # the contract alone; reads no project\nnpm run crm -- solution check    <plan.json>   # validate, then bind to this project's app inspect report\n```\n\nWrite the plan, record the `app inspect` report you read in step 2 into `application`, and run `check` before writing any code. Fix every problem it reports. Re-run it before the review: a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`, and a stale plan is not a plan.\n\nWhat the contract makes non-negotiable, and why each one is there:\n\n- **six decision types**, one per rung of the hierarchy below — `configure`, `extend`, `evolve`, `provider`, `create-package`, `propose-kernel-capability`. Say which rungs you tried in `rungsTried`.\n- **`propose-kernel-capability` may never appear in `steps[]`.** Rung 5 is a proposal you write. Putting it in steps is refused (`PLAN_DECISION_NOT_A_STEP`) because patching the kernel to make a solution fit is exactly what the hierarchy exists to prevent.\n- **six evidence categories and no others**, and every derived metric, inference and recommendation must cite what it follows from. A missing category is a problem too — an omitted gap is a claim.\n- **approval codes are a closed set.** A `provider` step must carry `install_or_configure_provider`; the validator adds the requirement rather than trusting you to remember it.\n- **citations point one way.** A fact and an assumption cite nothing; a derived metric cites facts and assumptions; an inference adds derived metrics; a recommendation adds inferences. Unavailable evidence is never a source. The graph is acyclic by construction, so you cannot cite a conclusion as a premise even by accident.\n- **rung 3 and above must show their work.** `provider`, `create-package` and `propose-kernel-capability` each require every lower rung in `rungsTried`, a reason per rung in `rejectedRungs`, and the capability `gap`. Skipping the inspection is how a domain that already exists gets duplicated.\n- **the composition fingerprint is derived, not written.** Record the `inspectionFingerprint` that `solution check --json` reports. It is a drift detector, not proof of anything — but it is not a label you compose either, and a free-text value is refused.\n- **unknown keys are refused.** If the contract has no field for what you want to say, say it in the fields that exist rather than inventing one.\n- **a plan carries no command.** A step names a decision and the seam it uses. Anything that looks like something to run — a shell command, a URL to fetch, a substitution, a script tag — is refused (`PLAN_EXECUTABLE_CONTENT`). That filter is defense in depth; the real boundary is that the shape has no field anything reads as an instruction. Nothing executes a plan, here or anywhere in this framework.\n\nThe plan is what the human reviews. Code that arrives before the plan cannot be reviewed as a solution — only as a diff.\n\n## 6. Distinguish implemented from planned, every time\n\nState clearly which parts of the solution exist, which are in an open PR, and which are roadmap only. **Never present Marketing, Analytics Studio, attribution, durable automation or Cloud capabilities as available.** If the goal needs one of them, say what can be built now, what cannot, and what the missing piece blocks.\n\n## 7. Define the user's experience, not just the data\n\nWhich Admin views, what each shows, what a reader can conclude from it, and where the limitations are stated **on the screen** — a data-quality panel next to a conversion rate is part of the deliverable.\n\n## 8. Define acceptance before building\n\nQuality Gates · `crm package validate` · unit, integration and E2E tests · a clean-clone run · the browser smoke where available · and the exact JTBD row the solution claims to move, with the evidence that would justify it. A row moves only with linked evidence; \"the data model exists\" is not \"the job is done\".\n\n## 9. Ask for approval only at sensitive boundaries\n\nProceed without asking for analysis, proposals, generation, previews and tests. Require a human for: publishing to production · sending any external communication · activating a journey · changing a live audience · using sensitive data · launching ads · creating or increasing spend · installing or configuring a provider · changing secrets · auto-applying an experiment winner · anything irreversible or destructive.\n\nReal approval **roles** need the Production Spine. Until then this is a human-actor boundary, not RBAC — never describe it as secure.\n\n## The decision hierarchy — try each rung before the next\n\n```text\n1. configure an existing package        a policy version, a config value, a view\n2. extend through a declared seam       an action, a policy, a record on a package that owns the domain\n3. add or configure a provider          when the gap is an integration, not a model\n4. create a custom package              when no installed package owns the domain\n5. propose a kernel capability          ONLY with generic, multi-domain evidence, as an ADR discussion\n```\n\nSay which rungs you tried. Rung 5 is a proposal you write, never a step you take inside a solution.\n\n## Evidence-first output, in this order\n\n```text\nObserved facts        what the data says, with the query and its version\nDerived metrics       what was computed, and how\nAssumptions           what was taken as true without evidence\nInferences            what follows from facts plus assumptions\nRecommendations       what to do, traceable to the rows above\nUnavailable evidence  what could not be checked, and why\n```\n\nNo attribution model is causal truth. Where a valid control group exists, report **lift** as the better answer than attribution.\n\n## After the build\n\n```text\nobserve → diagnose → recommend → propose next version\n```\n\n**No silent operational change.** A recommendation is a proposal; applying it is a new plan, a new approval where required, and a new version.\n\n## 10. Finish with evidence, not code\n\nReport: what was built, how it works, the assumptions, the limitations, the Admin views, the tests and their results, the JTBD rows moved and why, and the approvals still outstanding. Run the adversarial review (`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §5) before any merge.\n\nFinish with `npm run verify` and the starter (`node examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`).\n\n## Never\n\nInvent a command, a provider, a package or a capability that does not exist · claim an unimplemented capability is available · claim a proposed MCP tool surface exists · move a JTBD row without linked evidence · patch the kernel to make a solution fit · refactor a legacy domain out of `packages/core` on the way past · send, publish or spend without an explicit human approval · describe any of this as sandboxed or authenticated.\n\n\nARGUMENTS: Put a partner-management area onto disk in this repository. The goal: manage business partners as first-class records with their own lifecycle in the CRM, distinct from the existing delivery-handover \"partner engagement\" reference snapshot (which is intentionally minimal — a name/reference on a delivery project, no SLA, no revenue share, no portal). Discover what already exists (packages/delivery's partner engagement, examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard) before deciding whether this is a new module, an extension of an existing package, or a new domain package. Follow AGENTS.md's required workflow (read PRODUCT.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, DECISIONS.md, relevant skill; ExecPlan for multi-file work; npm run verify at the end)."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"efebce24-9b91-43c6-aefa-081fcb6e5714","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:51:29.092Z","isSynthetic":true}

There is no\n7\tprivate extension mechanism, no plugin API that only Anthropic-shaped packages\n8\tget, and no marketplace: a package is checked-in source in your own repository,\n9\tregistered by one static import.\n10\t\n11\tReference implementations, in increasing size:\n12\t\n13\t| Package | What it shows |\n14\t|---|---|\n15\t| [`examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard`](../examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard) | the smallest honest package: one resource, one action, one policy — **written as a customer would write it** |\n16\t| [`packages/delivery`](../packages/delivery) | a package that **depends on another package** through a declared capability |\n17\t| [`packages/contracts`](../packages/contracts) | a package that **offers** a capability to others |\n18\t\n19\t## 1. First decide whether you need a package at all\n20\t\n21\tMost requests do not.\n22\t\n23\t| You need | Use |\n24\t|---|---|\n25\t| one new object with fields, CRUD, API, Admin and tests | the **module factory** — `npm run crm -- module create <manifest>.json --apply` (`docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md`) |\n26\t| one lifecycle step on an existing record | a **record action** in `packages/actions/generated/index.js` (`docs/ACTIONS.md`) |\n27\t| one versioned business rule | a **policy** on an existing registry (scoring, discount, activation…) |\n28\t| a bounded domain: several related records, its own invariants, its own actions and evidence, installable and removable as a unit | a **domain package** |\n29\t\n30\tA package is justified when it owns **all** of these:\n31\t\n32\t- more than one related resource;\n33\t- lifecycle or business invariants that must hold across them;\n34\t- actions or workflows that enforce those invariants;\n35\t- evidence records that explain what happened and why;\n36\t- a meaningful \"install it / remove it\" boundary.\n37\t\n38\tA single custom object with a couple of fields is a module. Making it a package\n39\tadds a registration file, a README, a schema block and a conformance suite in\n40\texchange for nothing.\n41\t\n42\t**Do not** create a package to hold shared helpers, to work around a kernel\n43\tlimitation, or to \"organize\" code you already have. If you need a kernel change\n44\tto make your package work, that is a missing runtime capability: raise it as\n45\tone rather than reaching into `packages/core/src`.\n46\t\n47\t### Then start from a scaffold, not from somebody else's domain\n48\t\n49\t```bash\n50\tnpm run crm -- package scaffold field-service            # a plan; writes nothing\n51\tnpm run crm -- package scaffold field-service --apply    # two files\n52\t```\n53\t\n54\tYou get exactly two files — `src/index.js` and `README.md` — with an identity, a\n55\tcontract version and five **empty** declarations. That is already a conforming\n56\tpackage: `crm package test packages/field-service` exits 0 on it before you have\n57\ttyped anything, so every edit you make from here starts from a known-good\n58\tbaseline instead of from a domain you then have to subtract.\n59\t\n60\tIt deliberately generates **no** record, action, policy, capability, provider,\n61\tAdmin section, Solution Plan or MCP tool. A generated `status` field is a\n62\tdecision about a business nobody described, and a decision you have to notice\n63\tbefore you can delete it is worse than an empty list. It also does not compose\n64\tthe package, run a migration, open a database or install anything — see §12.\n65\t\n66\tAn occupied directory is refused rather than overwritten, and a name the\n67\tregistry would reject is refused **with a suggestion** rather than quietly\n68\trenamed. It checks the target directory, not the composed application: a name\n69\talready registered by another package is refused at startup by the registry, and\n70\t`crm app inspect --json` is what shows you that once the package is composed.\n71\t\n72\tTwo things worth knowing before you automate it. A plan **reserves nothing** —\n73\t`--apply` re-checks the target and answers `TARGET_CLAIMED` if something got\n74\tthere first. And because a plan and an apply both exit 0, read **`modeReason`**\n75\trather than the exit code to learn whether anything was written; an explicit\n76\t`--dry-run` beats `--apply`. An interrupted earlier run blocks nothing: its\n77\tstaging directory is reported as `staleStaging` for you to remove, never\n78\tdeleted automatically.\n79\t\n80\t`--into <dir>` puts the package somewhere other than `packages/`; `--json`\n81\tgives an agent the plan, its file hashes and a `fingerprint`.\n82\tExecPlan: `docs/plans/dx3-package-scaffold.md`.\n83\t\n84\t## 2. Pick a canonical identity\n85\t\n86\t```js\n87\tpackageContract: 1        // the contract you are written against\n88\tname: 'partner-scorecard' // lowercase, digits and hyphens; unique in the app\n89\tversion: 1                // your package's own version, a positive integer\n90\tlabel: 'Partner scorecard'\n91\t```\n92\t\n93\tThe name is a Map key in the registry and a key in `/api/schema`. It is also\n94\twhat a collision is reported against, so choose something a stranger reading a\n95\tstack trace would recognise.\n96\t\n97\t## 3. Declare what you need from other packages\n98\t\n99\tA package may reach another package **only** through a declared capability:\n100\t\n101\t```js\n102\trequires: [{ package: 'contracts', capability: 'delivery-obligations', version: 1 }],\n103\t```\n104\t\n105\tThe registry resolves this at startup. A missing package, a capability the\n106\tnamed package does not offer, a version mismatch or a dependency cycle stops\n107\tthe application with the missing edge named — never at runtime, inside a\n108\ttransaction.\n109\t\n110\tTo open it (inside an action, where you have `domains`):\n111\t\n112\t```js\n113\tconst obligations = domains.capability({\n114\t  consumer: 'delivery',\n115\t  capability: 'delivery-obligations',\n116\t  version: 1,\n117\t  context: { modules, actor, now },   // YOUR runtime handles\n118\t});\n119\t```\n120\t\n121\tBecause the capability is created with the caller's `modules`, everything it\n122\treads and writes happens **inside the caller's transaction** — a cross-package\n123\tcommit is atomic without either package sharing a database handle. A package\n124\tthat did not declare the requirement is refused even when the capability exists\n125\t(`CAPABILITY_NOT_DECLARED`), and a declaration naming the wrong provider is\n126\trefused too (`CAPABILITY_PROVIDER_MISMATCH`). The registry hands out no\n127\tdefinition and no mutable index, so there is no second route to the same\n128\tinterface — see \"What the contract does and does not enforce\" below for the\n129\tline this stops at.\n130\t\n131\tOffering one is the mirror image:\n132\t\n133\t```js\n134\tcapabilities: [{\n135\t  name: 'delivery-obligations',\n136\t  version: 1,\n137\t  description: 'Read pending delivery obligations and mark them handed over.',\n138\t  create({ modules, actor, now }) { return { /* the bounded interface */ }; },\n139\t}],\n140\t```\n141\t\n142\tExpose the smallest interface that does the job. `packages/contracts` offers\n143\tthree methods and no service, table or query handle — that is the standard.\n144\t\n145\t## 4. Create package-owned resources\n146\t\n147\tRecords are ordinary manifests, applied by the ordinary module factory:\n148\t\n149\t```bash\n150\tnpm run crm -- module create packages/<name>/modules/<record>.module.json --apply\n151\t```\n152\t\n153\tDeclare them on the package so a collision is detectable before boot:\n154\t\n155\t```js\n156\tresources: ['delivery-project', 'delivery-work-package', 'delivery-milestone'],\n157\t```\n158\t\n159\tTwo packages claiming one resource is a startup failure. For evidence records —\n160\tanything a package writes as the record of a decision — make every field\n161\t`\"writable\": \"managed\"`: the module then exposes only `get`/`list` publicly and\n162\texists solely through your actions.\n163\t\n164\t### Acting on a record you do not own\n165\t\n166\tA package often needs to act on a record the **project** owns — a `lead`, a\n167\t`company`, an `opportunity`. That is a **host-record dependency**, and it is a\n168\tdifferent thing from depending on another package.\n169\t\n170\t**Do not list it in `resources`.** `resources` declares what your package\n171\t*owns*, and ownership is what makes a second claimant a startup collision. A\n172\tpackage that lists `lead` is claiming the project's record, which is both untrue\n173\tand a conflict waiting for the next package that does the same.\n174\t\n175\t| | Host-record dependency | Package dependency |\n176\t|---|---|---|\n177\t| Example | Intelligence's `score` action targets the project's `lead` | Delivery reads Contracts' obligations |\n178\t| Declared as | nothing — the action names the module it targets | `requires: [{ package, capability, version }]` |\n179\t| Reached through | the action runtime, which hands you the record and a managed write path | `domains.capability(...)` |\n180\t| If it is missing | the action is simply not registered on a module the project does not have | startup fails, naming the unmet edge |\n181\t| Listed in `resources` | **no** | no — the *provider* lists its own |\n182\t\n183\tThe practical test: *if the customer deleted this record type, would my package\n184\tbe wrong, or just unused?* If the record is the project's and your package would\n185\tsimply have nothing to act on, it is a host-record dependency. If your package\n186\twould be broken because it needed somebody else's *behaviour*, that is a\n187\tcapability, and it must be declared.\n188\t\n189\tRemoving a package must leave host records untouched. Lead Intelligence's\n190\textraction is the worked example: it owns seven evidence records, declares\n191\t`lead` in none of them, and removing the package leaves every Lead intact —\n192\tproved in `tests/intelligence-package-absence.test.js`.\n193\t\n194\t## 5. Write actions and policies\n195\t\n196\tAn action is an ordinary action definition (`docs/ACTIONS.md`): the same\n197\truntime, transaction, audit, events and trace. Nothing about it is special\n198\tbecause it came from a package.\n199\t\n200\tA versioned decision belongs in a policy with declared JSON-safe `config`, so\n201\tits fingerprint (ADR-015) covers the thresholds as well as the code:\n202\t\n203\t```js\n204\tpolicies: [{ kind: 'partner-rating-policy', definition: standardPartnerRatingV1 }],\n205\t```\n206\t\n207\tRegistering a changed source or config for an existing version stops the next\n208\tboot. Publishing a new version is how a decision changes — the old one still\n209\texplains the records it produced.\n210\t\n211\tPolicy handlers must be deterministic, synchronous and total: no clock, no\n212\tnetwork, no database, no randomness, and a deep-frozen input.\n213\t\n214\t## 6. Publish only bounded metadata\n215\t\n216\t```js\n217\tmetadata() {\n218\t  return { scorecardContract: 1, ratings: ['preferred', 'approved', 'watch'], notModeled: [...] };\n219\t}\n220\t```\n221\t\n222\t`metadata()` is called once at startup and must return plain data. No function,\n223\tno credential, no file path, no raw payload — it is served to every client at\n224\t`/api/schema`. State what your package does **not** do: a `notModeled` list is\n225\thow a reader learns the limits without reading your source.\n226\t\n227\t## 7. Admin\n228\t\n229\tThe generated Admin renders package resources automatically (list, detail,\n230\tread-only fields). Write a view only when a generic form genuinely cannot do\n231\tthe job — a plan-then-confirm flow, a decision that needs its reason next to\n232\tit. When you do, follow the existing rules: text renders as text, the server\n233\towns every amount, controls disable while a request is in flight, and the\n234\tsection renders **only** when `/api/schema` publishes your package.\n235\t\n236\t## 8. Register statically\n237\t\n238\tOne import, in the checked-in composition file:\n239\t\n240\t```js\n241\t// packages/domains/generated/index.js\n242\timport { createPartnerScorecardPackage } from '../../../examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/src/index.js';\n243\t\n244\texport const generatedDomains = [\n245\t  createContractsDomain({ policies: [b2bSaasOrderActivationV1] }),\n246\t  createPartnerScorecardPackage(),\n247\t];\n248\t```\n249\t\n250\tThat file is the only place a project names its packages. Deleting the line\n251\tremoves the package: its actions disappear from the schema, its Admin section\n252\tdisappears with them, and its data is left alone — the framework never drops\n253\tyour tables behind you.\n254\t\n255\tThere is **no** dynamic loading, no request-controlled import, no `eval`, no\n256\tremote install and no marketplace. A package is source you can read in your own\n257\trepository, and that is the security model.\n258\t\n259\t### What the contract does and does not enforce\n260\t\n261\tThe package contract is **fail-closed against accidents**, not a sandbox\n262\tagainst hostile code. Concretely, it enforces:\n263\t\n264\t- the registry keeps its own indexes private, so no package can mutate the\n265\t  composition at runtime;\n266\t- `domains.get(name)` returns a frozen public summary — never a definition, and\n267\t  never another package's `create()` or policy handlers;\n268\t- a capability opens only for a consumer that declared it, from the package the\n269\t  declaration named;\n270\t- `metadata()` may add to the schema block but may never restate what the\n271\t  registry computes (`version`, `resources`, `requires`, `provides`, `actions`,\n272\t  `policies`…), and must be plain, function-free data.\n273\t\n274\tIt does **not** enforce, and must not be described as if it did:\n275\t\n276\t- the consumer name passed when opening a capability is asserted by the caller.\n277\t  A package that deliberately names another package as the consumer is a\n278\t  trusted-source problem, not something the runtime can distinguish;\n279\t- nothing sandboxes a package's module body, its actions or its policies. They\n280\t  run in-process with full authority;\n281\t- `package validate` executes the source it validates (see §9).\n282\t\n283\t## 9. Validate before you boot\n284\t\n285\t```bash\n286\tnpm run crm -- package validate packages/delivery\n287\tnpm run crm -- package inspect packages/delivery\n288\t```\n289\t\n290\tBoth run the same validator the application runs at startup and exit non-zero\n291\ton any problem. `inspect` adds the function-free metadata the schema would\n292\tpublish. Neither writes a file, opens a database or reaches the network, and\n293\tneither prints an absolute path.\n294\t\n295\t**They are not static analysis, and not a sandbox.** Reading a code-first\n296\tdefinition means importing your `src/index.js`, so your module body runs with\n297\tthe command's full authority — it can write files, open sockets and read the\n298\tenvironment, exactly as it would at boot. That is the same trust boundary as\n299\tthe composition file: repository source is trusted. Point these commands only\n300\tat a package you would be willing to boot.\n301\t\n302\tThey check identity and contract version, resource and capability collisions,\n303\tduplicate policy identities, dependency declarations — and that no file in your\n304\tpackage imports a private kernel path, in any quote style and through\n305\t`import()` and `require()` as well as a static `from` clause.\n306\t\n307\t## 10. The public kernel surface\n308\t\n309\tA package imports from **`packages/core/index.js`** and nothing else under\n310\t`packages/core`:\n311\t\n312\t```js\n313\timport { definePackage, AppError, ValidationError, requiredString } from '../../core/index.js';\n314\t```\n315\t\n316\tWhat is public today: the package contract (`definePackage`,\n317\t`validatePackageDefinition`, `PackageRegistry`), the error types, the\n318\tdeclared-definition fingerprint helpers, the money helpers and bounds, and the\n319\tshared value validators.\n320\t\n321\tEverything in `packages/core/src/*` is **private**. It changes without notice,\n322\tand `package validate` fails a package that reaches into it. If you need\n323\tsomething that is not exported, say so in an issue: the answer is either a new\n324\tpublic export or a missing runtime capability, never a deep import.\n325\t\n326\tWithin your own package, import freely — but never reach into another\n327\t*package's* private source either. That is what capabilities are for.\n328\t\n329\t## 11. Prove conformance mechanically\n330\t\n331\t```bash\n332\tnpm run crm -- package test packages/<your-package> --json\n333\t```\n334\t\n335\t`crm package test` (DX4) answers one question — **does this package satisfy the\n336\tframework's generic package contract and integration invariants?** — by\n337\tcomposing it into a throwaway copy of your project and booting an application\n338\ttwice, once with it and once without.\n339\t\n340\tIt is not the same question as the other three:\n341\t\n342\t```text\n343\tpackage scaffold   give me an empty package that already conforms\n344\tpackage validate   is this declaration structurally valid?\n345\tpackage inspect    what does this package declare, own, offer and need?\n346\tpackage test       does it hold up when a real application composes it?\n347\t```\n348\t\n349\tWhat it proves, from the framework's own machinery rather than a second\n350\timplementation of it: the declaration validates and the contract version is\n351\tsupported · metadata is data, function-free, bounded and identical twice · every\n352\tpolicy carries a fingerprint · no source reaches into `packages/core/src` or\n353\tanother package's private `src/` · no `eval`, `new Function` or dynamic import ·\n354\ta duplicate registration, a resource collision, a capability collision, **every**\n355\tunmet dependency and an undeclared reach are all refused · every module manifest\n356\tapplies with a valid state file and unique migration identities · the\n357\tapplication boots with the package, registers every declared action against a\n358\treal generated module and opens every declared capability · `crm app inspect`\n359\tdescribes the same package the declaration does · and the whole surface\n360\tdisappears when the package is removed from the composition.\n361\t\n362\tEvery check row names the **authority** it speaks for — `package-contract`,\n363\t`composition`, `authoring-rule`, `module-factory`, `application-boot` or\n364\t`app-inspect`. There is deliberately no `dx4` authority: a rule this command\n365\twould have had to invent is either advisory or absent, because a conformance kit\n366\tthat invents rules is a second, undocumented package contract.\n367\t\n368\t**One rule is worth reading before you write an action.** If your action targets\n369\ta record another *package* owns, declare a capability of that package in\n370\t`requires`. Without it your package cannot be composed into any project that\n371\tlacks the owner, and nothing in your declaration says so — `package test` fails\n372\tit with `UNDECLARED_PACKAGE_RECORD_DEPENDENCY` and names the capabilities the\n373\towner does offer. Records that **no** package owns are different: those belong to\n374\tthe host application, every package here acts on `order`, and depending on them\n375\tneeds no declaration.\n376\t\n377\tWhat it deliberately does **not** prove is listed by code in every report:\n378\t`DOMAIN_CORRECTNESS_NOT_PROVEN` first among them. No action is executed, no\n379\tpolicy is evaluated, no state transition is driven and no provider is contacted.\n380\tThose are your package's own tests.\n381\t\n382\t**Trusted source, isolated execution, not a sandbox.** Every import and every\n383\tboot happens in a child process, in its own process group, under a timeout, with\n384\tthe report on file descriptor 3 so a package that prints cannot corrupt it. Your\n385\tproject is never written to and your database is never opened. The child still\n386\tholds your authority.\n387\t\n388\tExit codes: `0` conforms · `1` conformance failures · `2` the package or the\n389\tproject could not be read.\n390\t\n391\t## 12. Tests, example and evidence\n392\t\n393\t- Reuse `tests/helpers/package-conformance.js` for the checks every package\n394\t  shares: contract metadata, public imports only, dependency resolution,\n395\t  deterministic function-free schema, collision handling. It and\n396\t  `crm package test` share one private-import rule and one source walk\n397\t  (`packages/cli/src/package-sources.js`), so they cannot drift apart.\n398\t- Add an end-to-end test that boots a real project with your package applied\n399\t  and drives your action over the real HTTP/SDK path.\n400\t- Prove optionality: the same project without your package must boot and\n401\t  behave identically.\n402\t- Update `docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` **conservatively**. A row moves\n403\t  only with linked evidence, and \"the data model exists\" is not \"the job is\n404\t  done\".\n405\t- Ship a README next to your package: what it owns, what it needs, how to\n406\t  enable it, and what it deliberately does not do.\n407\t\n408\t## 13. Submit for review\n409\t\n410\tThe whole path, end to end:\n411\t\n412\t```text\n413\tcrm app inspect        what does this application already have?      (AX1)\n414\tcrm solution check     does my plan still match it?                  (AX2)\n415\tcrm package scaffold   an empty package that already conforms        (DX3)\n416\t  edit                 records, actions, capabilities, policies — by hand\n417\tcrm module create      each record you own, from its manifest\n418\tcrm package validate   is the declaration structurally valid?\n419\tcrm package inspect    what does it declare, own, offer and need?\n420\tcrm package test       does it hold up when an application composes it? (DX4)\n421\t  your own tests       is the DOMAIN right? nothing above answers this\n422\t  compose              one import, added by hand, deliberately\n423\t```\n424\t\n425\tRun `crm package test` on your package, `npm run verify` from a clean clone, run the starter, then open a PR and\n426\tleave it open for the adversarial review in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §5. The\n427\treview will attack your package's boundary, its atomicity and its claims — the\n428\tsame way it attacks first-party ones.\n429\t\n430\t\n431\t## 14. Official packages are reference implementations, not a framework tax\n432\t\n433\tThe first-party packages (`contracts`, `delivery`, and the planned Marketing\n434\tpackages in `docs/strategy/MARKETING_GROWTH_OPERATIONS.md`) attach through the\n435\tcontract on this page and no other. That has a consequence worth stating:\n436\t\n437\t- an official package is **optional** — take it, or don't;\n438\t- you may **replace** one with your own. Keep `marketing`, write your own\n439\t  `journeys`; or collapse the lot into a single custom Growth package for your\n440\t  business;\n441\t- you may write **custom channel or provider packages** for anything nobody\n442\t  upstream has adapted, with no kernel patch;\n443\t- you may **extend** any package with your own resources, actions and policies.\n444\t\n445\tA package that needs consent state, funnel metrics or durable enrolment declares\n446\ta **capability** for it — governance, analytics, durable automation — rather than\n447\treaching into another package's tables. If the capability does not exist yet,\n448\tthat is a missing runtime capability to raise, not a deep import to write.\n449\t\n450\tTwo rules no package may bend, official or custom: **nothing bypasses human\n451\tapproval, consent, audit or the provider boundary**, and **trusted checked-in\n452\tsource is not sandboxed** (ADR-018 addendum 4). A package that sends, publishes\n453\tor spends without a recorded human approval is a defect regardless of who wrote\n454\tit.\n455\t\n456\t*(No Marketing package exists today. The packages named above are planned\n457\tidentities, not shipped code, and a future Marketing authoring Skill is planned\n458\trather than implemented.)*\n459\t\n460\t## 15. When an agent is working from a business goal\n461\t\n462\tA package is often the answer an agent reaches when a user states an *objective*\n463\trather than a change — \"track our funnel by acquisition channel\" resolves to\n464\treused packages plus, sometimes, one new custom package. The rules do not\n465\tchange, and two of them matter more in that mode:\n466\t\n467\t- **Discover before you create.** `crm app inspect --json` (AX1) says what this\n468\t  application already is; `crm package inspect` and `GET /api/schema` say what\n469\t  one package provides. Duplicating an existing domain is the most common\n470\t  failure, and it is the one inspection exists to prevent.\n471\t- **Report what you could not build.** A package that silently omits the part\n472\t  of the goal it had no capability for is worse than one that names the gap.\n473\t\n474\tSee `docs/strategy/OBJECTIVE_DRIVEN_AGENT_EXPERIENCE.md` and the\n475\t`solve-business-goal` Skill.\n476\t\n477\t## Moving an existing domain into a package\n478\t\n479\tThree domains — Lead Intelligence, Commercial Operations, and Signature & Order\n480\t— predate this seam and still live in `packages/core/src/`. Their status against\n481\tevery horizontal capability is recorded in\n482\t`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` as `needs_extraction`: the gap is\n483\tstructural, and no amount of care inside a core file closes it.\n484\t\n485\t**Do not extract one as part of another piece of work.** A behavior-preserving\n486\textraction is its own PR, its own review and its own acceptance criterion, and\n487\tit is sequenced:\n488\t\n489\t```text\n490\t1.  M14b2, reviewed and merged                                   done\n491\t2.  M15 — Service, built on this seam                            done\n492\t3.  review the M15 learnings: what the seam still cannot express done\n493\t4.  DX4 (`crm package test`) — mechanical conformance            done\n494\t5.  DX3 (`crm package scaffold`) — a conforming starting point   done\n495\t6.  one controlled extraction, one domain, one PR                not started\n496\t```\n497\t\n498\tSteps 3 and 4 are not ceremony. Contracts and Delivery are two data points and\n499\tonly Delivery has *consumed* another package's capability; Service is the third\n500\tand the first built with a mature seam. And an extraction whose only proof is\n501\t\"the existing tests still pass\" is exactly the proof that misses a boundary\n502\tviolation — DX4 is what makes conformance mechanical rather than argued.\n503\t\n504\tWhen the time comes, the acceptance criterion is **behavior preservation, proved\n505\tfrom the outside**: every historical decision the domain recorded still\n506\treproduces identically, the same actions answer on the same routes, the audit\n507\tand trace shape is unchanged, and the package detaches cleanly. A \"cleaner\"\n508\textraction that changes one recorded outcome has failed.\n509\t\n510\t`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` records Lead Intelligence as the\n511\t**working hypothesis** for the first extraction, with the evidence that must\n512\texist first. It is a hypothesis, and nothing in this guide authorizes starting.\n513\t\n514\t## What is deliberately not here yet\n515\t\n516\t- **A scaffold that generates domain semantics.** `crm package scaffold` ships\n517\t  (§1), and it deliberately stops at an identity. A record still needs a\n518\t  manifest and `crm module create`; an action, a capability and a policy are\n519\t  still yours to write. Generating the wrong skeleton into every customer\n520\t  repository is harder to undo than typing two files.\n521\t- **A registry, npm publication, remote install, auto-update, signing or a\n522\t  marketplace.** Packages are checked-in source. Distribution is a separate\n523\t  problem with a separate threat model, and none of it is needed to author a\n524\t  package today.\n525\t- **Hot loading.** Registration is static and composed at startup, on purpose.\n526\t\n527\t## Seeing your package in the application\n528\t\n529\t`crm package validate|inspect <dir>` reads one package in isolation. Once it is\n530\tregistered in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`, `crm app inspect --json`\n531\tshows it as part of the whole composition — its resolved capability edges, the\n532\trecords it owns and their revisions, its actions and its policy fingerprints —\n533\tand reports every collision, missing dependency or cycle deterministically\n534\trather than stopping at the first fault. Guide:\n535\t`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`.\n536\t\n537\t## Related\n538\t\n539\t`ARCHITECTURE.md` (domain packages) · ADR-018 and its addenda in `DECISIONS.md`\n540\t· `docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md` · `docs/ACTIONS.md` · `docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md` ·\n541\t`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` · `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` ·\n542\t`docs/architecture/AGENT_TOOL_SURFACE.md` ·\n543\t`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md` (mirrored at\n544\t`.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`).\n545\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"87dcdc3f-4c25-42d9-8dfb-9dce2e6402d0","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:07.604Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LDkUTHPth2U6USEfYWVsYq","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: build-custom-domain-package\n3\tdescription: Create or extend a domain package in an Accordo repository - a bounded domain with its own resources, actions, policies, capabilities and schema metadata, registered statically and removable without touching the kernel. Use for \"add a custom package\", package contract, package dependency/capability, package validation or new-domain work, including customer-specific packages. Do not use for a single custom object (create-crm-module) or one lifecycle step on an existing record (create-crm-workflow).\n4\trequires:\n5\t  tier: generated-project\n6\t  command: \"crm app inspect\"\n7\t  projectSurface: [\"packages/domains/generated/index.js\", \"packages/core/index.js\", \"examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard\"]\n8\t  repositorySurface: [\"docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md\", \"DECISIONS.md\", \"docs/QUALITY_GATES.md\", \"docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md\"]\n9\t  degradesTo: \"`crm package validate <dir>` and `crm package inspect <dir>` — the same validator startup runs — plus the composition reported by `crm app inspect --json`\"\n10\t---\n11\t\n12\t## Orient yourself first\n13\t\n14\t```bash\n15\tnpm run crm -- app inspect --json\n16\t```\n17\t\n18\tRead `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]`, in that order. Every problem is fixed or reported before anything is built on top of it, and **every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim.** Then read `packages[]`, `capabilities[]`, `resources[]`, `actions[]`, `policies[]` and `providers[]`: that list is what exists. A capability absent from the report does not exist, whatever a record name, a label or a document suggests.\n19\t\n20\tIf the repository documents this skill names are absent, you are in a project built from this framework rather than in the framework itself. The inspection report is then the source of truth and those documents are optional background — do not guess at their contents, and do not assume a path exists because this skill names it.\n21\t\n22\tThe package contract itself is enforced by a command, not by a document: `npm run crm -- package validate <dir>` runs the same validator startup runs, and its problems are the authority on whether a package is well-formed.\n23\t\n24\t**Background, where they exist:** `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` and `DECISIONS.md` (ADR-018 and its addenda). The reference packages, where the project carries them, are `examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard` (smallest, customer-authored), `packages/delivery` (depends on another package) and `packages/contracts` (offers a capability). They are the deeper source for the rules below, not a prerequisite for them — the rules stand on their own.\n25\t\n26\t## Decide whether it is a package at all\n27\t\n28\t1. One object with fields and CRUD → **module factory**, not a package. One lifecycle step on an existing record → **an action**. One versioned rule → **a policy**. Say so and do that instead; a package for a single object is overhead with no boundary.\n29\t2. A package is justified only when it owns several related resources, invariants across them, the actions that enforce those invariants, its own evidence records, and a real install/remove boundary.\n30\t3. Never create a package to hold shared helpers or to work around a kernel limitation.\n31\t\n32\t## Start from a scaffold\n33\t\n34\t1. `npm run crm -- package scaffold <name>` prints a plan and writes nothing; `--apply` writes exactly two files, `src/index.js` and `README.md`, with an identity and five **empty** declarations. That output already passes `package validate`, `package inspect` and `package test` — start from it rather than from a copy of somebody else's domain.\n35\t2. It generates **no** record, action, policy, capability, provider, Admin section, Solution Plan or MCP tool, and that is deliberate. Do not treat the empty lists as something to be filled by pattern-matching another package: every entry you add is a decision about this business.\n36\t3. It composes nothing, opens no database and runs no migration. An occupied directory is refused, never overwritten; an invalid name is refused **with a suggestion**, never silently renamed — if you get `PACKAGE_NAME_INVALID`, ask for the canonical name explicitly rather than assuming the suggestion.\n37\t4. `--into <dir>` places the package outside `packages/`; `--json` gives you the plan, its per-file hashes and a `fingerprint`. Exit 0 planned or applied, exit 1 refused — so **read `modeReason`, not the exit code**, to know whether anything was written; an explicit `--dry-run` beats `--apply` and still exits 0.\n38\t5. A plan reserves nothing. `--apply` re-checks the target and answers `TARGET_CLAIMED` if another process got there first. An interrupted earlier run blocks nothing: its staging is reported as `staleStaging` for you to remove, never deleted automatically and never treated as a lock.\n39\t6. The scaffold cannot tell you the identity is unique in the application — it checks the target directory only. Compose the package, then run `crm app inspect --json`.\n40\t\n41\t## Author it against the public contract\n42\t\n43\t1. Declare identity: `packageContract: 1`, a canonical `name` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`), a positive integer `version`, a bounded `label` and `description`.\n44\t2. Declare `resources` — the record modules you own. Two packages claiming one resource is a startup failure, and declaring them is what makes that detectable.\n45\t3. Import **only** from `packages/core/index.js`. Anything under `packages/core/src/*` is private; `package validate` fails a package that reaches into it. If something you need is not exported, that is a missing public export or a missing runtime capability — raise it, do not deep-import.\n46\t4. Records are ordinary manifests applied by the module factory. Evidence records are `\"writable\": \"managed\"` throughout, so they are `get`/`list` publicly and exist only through your actions.\n47\t5. Actions are ordinary action definitions: same runtime, transaction, audit, events and trace. Versioned decisions go in policies with declared JSON-safe `config` so the ADR-015 fingerprint covers them; handlers stay deterministic, synchronous and total.\n48\t6. `metadata()` returns plain data only — no function, credential or path. Include a `notModeled` list: the limits are part of the contract.\n49\t\n50\t## Cross-package dependencies\n51\t\n52\t1. Reach another package **only** through a declared capability: `requires: [{package, capability, version}]`, opened with `domains.capability({consumer, capability, version, context: {modules, actor, now}})`.\n53\t2. Pass your own runtime handles as the context, so the capability participates in **your** transaction. That is what makes a cross-package commit atomic without sharing a database handle.\n54\t3. Offer the smallest interface that does the job — methods, never a service, table or query handle. Version it; a new shape is a new version.\n55\t4. Never deep-import another package's source. An undeclared reach is refused at runtime and a cycle is refused at startup, but the rule is the design, not the guardrail.\n56\t\n57\t## Register, validate, prove\n58\t\n59\t1. Registration is one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. No dynamic import, no `eval`, no remote install, no marketplace, no hot loading.\n60\t2. Walk the three commands in order; they answer three different questions and none of them substitutes for another:\n61\t\n62\t```text\n63\tcrm package scaffold <name>  an empty package that already conforms\n64\tcrm package inspect <dir>    what does it declare, own, offer and need?\n65\tcrm package validate <dir>   is that declaration structurally valid?\n66\tcrm package test <dir>       does it hold up when a real application composes it?\n67\tnpm test -- <your suite>     does it do the right thing?   ← only your tests answer this\n68\tnpm run verify               does the whole project still hold?\n69\t```\n70\t\n71\t3. `crm package test` (DX4) composes your package into a throwaway copy of the project and boots it twice — with it and without it. It proves the declaration, the boundaries, every composition refusal, module manifests and migration identity, attach and detach, and that `app inspect` describes the same package you declared. **Generic conformance is not domain correctness**: no action is executed, no policy is evaluated and no state transition is driven. Every such gap is a named limitation in the report, starting with `DOMAIN_CORRECTNESS_NOT_PROVEN`. Your business JTBDs still need package-specific tests.\n72\t4. All three commands **import** your package, and `test` also boots it, so your code runs with the CLI's authority. The harness does not intentionally mutate the caller project, and `test` imports your package from a throwaway copy rather than from your tree — but process isolation protects the invoking process from crashes, global-state changes and hangs, and is **not** a filesystem, network or OS sandbox. Never describe it as one.\n73\t5. **Do not patch the kernel to make a check pass.** A conformance failure is a statement about your package. If you are certain it is a statement about the framework instead, say so in the PR with the exact check id and evidence — and if it is a horizontal gap, it goes through the Compatibility Backfill Rule in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`, not through a local fix.\n74\t6. If your action targets a record another **package** owns, declare a capability of that package in `requires`. Without it, `package test` fails you with `UNDECLARED_PACKAGE_RECORD_DEPENDENCY` and names what the owner offers — because your package cannot be composed into any project that lacks the owner, and nothing in your declaration says so. Records **no** package owns are different: they belong to the host application, every package here acts on `order`, and depending on them needs no declaration. If no capability of the owner expresses what you do, that is a real seam gap: say so in the PR rather than working around it.\n75\t7. Prove optionality: the same project without your package boots and behaves identically, and removing the import leaves the data alone.\n76\t8. Reuse the shared package-conformance helper where the project ships one (`tests/helpers/package-conformance.js` in this repository); otherwise write the same assertions yourself. Then add an end-to-end test that drives your action over the real HTTP/SDK path in a clean project.\n77\t9. Ship a README: what it owns, what it needs, how to enable it, what it deliberately does not do.\n78\t\n79\t## Claims discipline\n80\t\n81\tWhere the project carries a jobs matrix (`docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` in this repository), update it conservatively — a row moves only with linked evidence, and a data model is not a completed job. Where it carries none, the rule still holds against whatever the project claims in its README and its package metadata. Never describe a stored reference as access, permission or an account.\n82\t\n83\t## Do not implement here\n84\t\n85\tA package registry, npm publication, remote install, auto-update, cryptographic signing, a marketplace, hot loading, or any kernel patch. The scaffold (DX3) exists and stops at an identity on purpose — do not extend it to generate domain semantics, to edit `packages/domains/generated/index.js`, or to run a migration. See `docs/plans/dx3-package-scaffold.md`.\n86\t\n87\tFinish with `npm run verify`, the starter (`node examples/starters/b2b-lead-qualification/install.mjs`) and `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md`; leave the PR open for adversarial review.\n88\t\n89\t## What the contract enforces, and what it does not (ADR-018 addendum 4)\n90\t\n91\t1. The registry's indexes are private. `domains.get(name)` returns a **frozen public summary** — never a definition, never another package's `create()` or policy handlers. Do not look for a second route to an interface: there isn't one, and adding one is a kernel change.\n92\t2. A capability opens only for a consumer that declared it, from the package the declaration named. Declare the requirement; do not reach around it.\n93\t3. `metadata()` may add to your schema block but may **never restate** what the registry computes — `version`, `label`, `description`, `resources`, `requires`, `provides`, `actions`, `policies`. Those keys are refused at startup. It must return plain, function-free, JSON-safe data.\n94\t4. Package `name` is bounded (64 characters), lowercase-canonical, and Map-keyed.\n95\t5. Nothing here is a sandbox. `crm package validate` **imports and executes** your `src/index.js`; your actions and policies run in-process with full authority; and the consumer name passed when opening a capability is asserted by the caller. Never describe any of this as isolation.\n96\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"eacd94c8-4727-4ca1-87da-f54a557f0d26","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:07.952Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}

It exists to\n4\tprove that an agent — Claude Code or Codex — can add a bounded domain to a\n5\tcustomer's own repository through the public package contract, without\n6\ttouching the kernel.\n7\t\n8\tIt is deliberately the smallest thing that is still honestly a package: one\n9\tresource, one action, one versioned policy.\n10\t\n11\tRead [`docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`](../../../docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md) first.\n12\t\n13\t## What it models\n14\t\n15\tA human records how a delivery partner performed on an engagement: a score\n16\t(0–100), a required reason, and a rating band decided by a versioned policy.\n17\t\n18\tA rating is **internal opinion**. It grants nothing, changes nothing\n19\tcommercially, is never shown to the partner, and never rates anyone\n20\tautomatically.\n21\t\n22\t## What it proves\n23\t\n24\t| Claim | Where |\n25\t|---|---|\n26\t| package identity, version and contract version | `src/index.js` |\n27\t| a package-owned resource through the ordinary module factory | `modules/partner-scorecard.module.json` |\n28\t| a package-owned action on the generic action runtime | `buildRatePartnerAction()` |\n29\t| a versioned, fingerprinted policy with declared config | `standardPartnerRatingV1` |\n30\t| function-free schema metadata | `metadata()` |\n31\t| **public kernel imports only** | the single `packages/core/index.js` import |\n32\t| static registration, and removal by deleting one line | `packages/domains/generated/index.js` |\n33\t| no kernel change is needed for any of it | `tests/custom-package-e2e.test.js` fingerprints every kernel file before and after |\n34\t\n35\t## Enabling it\n36\t\n37\t```bash\n38\tnpm run crm -- module create examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/modules/partner-scorecard.module.json --apply\n39\tnpm run crm -- package validate examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard\n40\t```\n41\t\n42\t```js\n43\t// packages/domains/generated/index.js\n44\timport { createPartnerScorecardPackage } from '../../../examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/src/index.js';\n45\t\n46\texport const generatedDomains = [\n47\t  /* …first-party packages… */\n48\t  createPartnerScorecardPackage(),\n49\t];\n50\t```\n51\t\n52\tIt attaches to `delivery-partner-engagement`, so it needs the delivery package\n53\tapplied — but it declares no capability requirement, because it only adds an\n54\taction to a record another package owns.
