{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PsfZqcu6V5foFiCbpf5EsH","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# `work` — tasks and activity evidence\n2\t\n3\tTwo records, three human actions and one declared capability.\n4\t\n5\t| | |\n6\t|---|---|\n7\t| Owns | `work-task`, `work-activity` |\n8\t| Requires | **nothing** |\n9\t| Offers | `follow-up@1` |\n10\t| Actions | `work-task.complete`, `work-task.cancel`, `work-task.add-note` |\n11\t| Policies | none |\n12\t| ADR | ADR-030 · ExecPlan `docs/plans/activity-task-operations.md` · guide `docs/WORK_TASKS.md` |\n13\t\n14\t## What it is\n15\t\n16\tA **Task** is a unit of human follow-up work arising from a business event. An\n17\t**Activity** is an append-only, user-facing entry on a subject's timeline. A\n18\tdomain action that has just decided something opens a follow-up **inside its own\n19\ttransaction**, and the work appears in one queue with one lifecycle instead of in\n20\ta new table per domain.\n21\t\n22\t## What it is not\n23\t\n24\tStated here, in `metadata().notModeled`, and asserted as absent by the tests:\n25\t\n26\t> no scheduler · no reminders · no due-date alerts · no recurring or repeating\n27\t> work · no calendar sync · no meetings or calls · no email, SMS, WhatsApp or\n28\t> chat · no notifications · **no assignment, ownership, workload or capacity** ·\n29\t> no RBAC · no subtasks, dependencies or projects · no attachments · **no\n30\t> unified cross-domain timeline** · no job queue · no delayed workflows.\n31\t\n32\tNothing in this package runs on a timer, sends anything, or tells anybody. A task\n33\tis only ever moved by a person.\n34\t\n35\t## Enable it\n36\t\n37\t```bash\n38\tnpm run crm -- module create packages/work/modules/work-task.module.json --apply\n39\tnpm run crm -- module create packages/work/modules/work-activity.module.json --apply\n40\t```\n41\t\n42\t```js\n43\t// packages/domains/generated/index.js\n44\timport { createWorkPackage } from '../../work/src/index.js';\n45\t\n46\texport const generatedDomains = [\n47\t  createWorkPackage(),\n48\t  // a consuming package opts in explicitly:\n49\t  createLifecyclePackage({ followUp: true }),\n50\t  createServicePackage({ policies: [...], followUp: true }),\n51\t];\n52\t```\n53\t\n54\tRemoving the `createWorkPackage()` line removes the actions, the capability and\n55\tthe schema block. **It leaves every row**: the framework never drops your tables.\n56\t\n57\t## The Task lifecycle\n58\t\n59\tAn explicit transition table, published in the schema and in `app inspect`:\n60\t\n61\t```text\n62\topen  →  completed        work.complete   (human actor)\n63\topen  →  cancelled        work.cancel     (human actor, reason required)\n64\tcompleted → nothing\n65\tcancelled → nothing\n66\t```\n67\t\n68\t- **`open → completed` is direct.** An `in_progress` state would change no read,\n69\t  no action and no refusal, so it would be a label that also forces a rule nobody\n70\t  asked for.\n71\t- **There is no reopen.** A repeated business round is a new task under a new\n72\t  source key. Un-completing work would need a second lifecycle this package does\n73\t  not have, and a `409 INVALID_STATE` naming the allowed moves is the honest\n74\t  answer instead.\n75\t- **`dueAt` is evidence only.** No clock changes a status. A task past its due\n76\t  date is still `open`, and stays open until a person moves it.\n77\t\n78\t## The Activity vocabulary\n79\t\n80\tClosed, four entries: `task_created`, `task_completed`, `task_cancelled`, `note`.\n81\t\n82\tThe first three are written in the same transaction as the transition they\n83\tdescribe. A `note` is the one place free text enters — bounded, control-character\n84\tfree, rendered as text and **sent nowhere**.\n85\t\n86\t**Activity is not the audit log.** Audit and trace stay the technical record of\n87\tevery write; this is the curated user-facing one. Nothing projects one into the\n88\tother, and nothing could: the event bus dispatches after commit (ADR-012), so a\n89\tprojection would either escape the originating transaction or need Jobs/Outbox,\n90\twhich does not exist.\n91\t\n92\t## The subject envelope\n93\t\n94\t```text\n95\tsubjectResource      \"lead\", \"service-escalation\", \"commercial-followup\"…\n96\tsubjectId            the record id\n97\tsubjectOwner         \"host\" | \"package\"\n98\tsubjectOwnerPackage  the owning package, when subjectOwner is \"package\"\n99\tsubjectLabel         a display snapshot, taken once, never refreshed\n100\tsourcePackage        who created the task (\"host\" for a host action)\n101\tsourceAction         which action\n102\t```\n103\t\n104\t`subjectOwner` keeps the Package Contract's distinction between a\n105\t**host/project-owned** record (a Lead: it belongs to the project) and a **foreign\n106\tpackage-owned** one (a `service-escalation`: it disappears with Service).\n107\t\n108\t**`SUBJECT_REFERENCE_NOT_ENFORCED`.** SQLite cannot enforce a foreign key whose\n109\ttarget table varies per row, and this package does not pretend it can. What makes\n110\tthe envelope trustworthy is *who wrote it*: the domain action that owns the source\n111\trecord, running on that record, in the transaction that is about to commit.\n112\tNothing stops that record being deleted afterwards, and a task whose subject is\n113\tgone still reads as a task. Work reports the envelope as recorded; it never\n114\tclaims the subject still exists.\n115\t\n116\t## `follow-up@1`\n117\t\n118\t```js\n119\trequires: [{ package: 'work', capability: 'follow-up', version: 1 }],\n120\t```\n121\t\n122\t```js\n123\tconst work = domains.capability({\n124\t  consumer: 'my-package',\n125\t  capability: 'follow-up',\n126\t  version: 1,\n127\t  context: { modules, actor, now },   // YOUR runtime handles\n128\t});\n129\t\n130\tconst { task, activity, replayed } = await work.createFollowUp({\n131\t  sourceKey: `my-package-thing:${record.id}`,   // business identity, never a clock\n132\t  title: 'Something a person must do',\n133\t  dueAt: null,                                   // evidence only, and optional\n134\t  subject: { resource: 'my-record', id: record.id, owner: 'package', ownerPackage: 'my-package' },\n135\t  source: { package: 'my-package', action: 'my-action' },\n136\t});\n137\t\n138\twork.findBySourceKey('my-package-thing:123');    // exact read, or null\n139\t```\n140\t\n141\tBecause the capability is created with **your** `modules` handle, both rows are\n142\twritten inside **your** transaction. If your action fails afterwards, the task\n143\tand its activity roll back with your own writes — and that is *checked*: a call\n144\tthat cannot prove an open transaction on the same connection is refused `500\n145\tWORK_TRANSACTION_REQUIRED` before the first write, so no half pair can exist\n146\t(ADR-030 addendum 1).\n147\t\n148\t`source.package` is bound to the consumer identity the registry resolved, not\n149\tread from your request body: asserting a different one is `403\n150\tWORK_SOURCE_PACKAGE_MISMATCH`. It is a narrowing, not authentication — see\n151\t`metadata().capability.bindingLimitation`.\n152\t\n153\t### Idempotency\n154\t\n155\t- **Same key, same payload** → the existing task and its creation activity,\n156\t  `replayed: true`. That is the lost-response retry, and it is safe however many\n157\t  times it happens.\n158\t- **Same key, any different semantic field** → `409 WORK_FOLLOW_UP_CONFLICT`,\n159\t  `details.conflictingFields` naming each one. The semantic identity is `title`,\n160\t  `dueAt`, `subjectResource`, `subjectId`, `subjectOwner`, `subjectOwnerPackage`,\n161\t  `sourcePackage` and `sourceAction`. Recorded work is never silently\n162\t  overwritten, and it is never handed to a different package under the same key.\n163\t- **Same key, only a different `subject.label`** → replayed. The label is the one\n164\t  field the contract declares a non-authoritative display snapshot, and the\n165\t  stored one is not refreshed.\n166\t- **A new business round** → a new key, and a new task. Repeated work is never\n167\t  collapsed.\n168\t- **There is no clock heuristic on a key.** An earlier cut refused any ISO\n169\t  instant and any run of 13+ digits, which also refused 13-digit customer\n170\t  numbers, provider event ids, 16-digit order numbers and business events whose\n171\t  scheduled instant is genuinely their identity. A generic capability cannot tell\n172\t  a moving key from a stable one by looking at it. **You** own a stable business\n173\t  identity — derive it from a committed record id, never from `now()`, and prove\n174\t  it with your own retry test, as every consumer here does in\n175\t  `tests/work-source-key-stability.test.js`.\n176\t- **An impossible `dueAt` is refused**, never rolled over: `2027-02-30` is a `400`\n177\t  rather than a row quietly stored as 2027-03-02.\n178\t- **`MANUAL_NOTES_ARE_NOT_IDEMPOTENT`** — a note carries no key, because two\n179\t  identical notes are two statements.\n180\t\n181\t### The host application is not a package\n182\t\n183\t`PackageRegistry.capability({ consumer })` resolves `consumer` against\n184\t**registered packages**. A host action in project source — the starter's\n185\t`qualify` — is not one, and cannot declare a requirement. Rather than weaken the\n186\tdeclaration check, which is the entire value of the seam, the project composes\n187\tthe package's exported creator directly, exactly as\n188\t`packages/domains/generated/index.js` composes the package itself:\n189\t\n190\t```js\n191\timport { createFollowUp } from '../../../../packages/work/src/index.js';\n192\tawait createFollowUp({ modules, actor, now }, { /* the same request */ });\n193\t```\n194\t\n195\tIt is the same code the capability closes over. One implementation, two callers,\n196\tno fallback. Published as `HOST_ACTIONS_CANNOT_DECLARE_CAPABILITIES`.\n197\t\n198\t## Human-actor boundary\n199\t\n200\t`complete`, `cancel` and `add-note` require `actor.type === 'user'`; an agent\n201\tactor is refused `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. **This is not RBAC, not\n202\tauthentication and not secure assignment** — a local caller sets its own actor\n203\theader. What it buys is that an agent cannot silently close somebody's work.\n204\t\n205\tAutomatic creation through `follow-up@1` does **not** require a human actor: the\n206\tconsuming action's own boundary already decided that, and re-deciding it here\n207\twould make Work an authorization layer, which it is not.\n208\t\n209\t## Migrating the bespoke Lead Task\n210\t\n211\tThe B2B starter used to ship its own `task` module (table `tasks`, a **required**\n212\t`leadId` reference, `status` in `open`/`done`, every field publicly writable). It\n213\tis gone, replaced rather than evolved: Module Evolution is additive and\n214\tforward-only, so a required reference to `leads` cannot be relaxed into a generic\n215\tsubject.\n216\t\n217\t**The old table is never touched.** Nothing here issues DDL against `tasks`, an\n218\texisting database still opens, and every historical row is still readable.\n219\t`packages/work/src/legacy-tasks.js` adopts them forward:\n220\t\n221\t```js\n222\timport { migrateLegacyTasks } from './packages/work/src/legacy-tasks.js';\n223\t\n224\tmigrateLegacyTasks({ database: app.database, modules: app.modules, actor, now });                 // dry-run plan\n225\tmigrateLegacyTasks({ database: app.database, modules: app.modules, actor, now }, { apply: true }); // writes\n226\t```\n227\t\n228\tDry-run by default, idempotent (`legacy-task:<id>`), `done → completed`, `leadId\n229\t→ subject { resource: 'lead', owner: 'host' }`, and a row whose status it cannot\n230\tmap is **refused and named** rather than guessed. A migrated `done` task carries\n231\tno completion actor or instant, because the legacy row recorded neither.\n232\t\n233\t## Related\n234\t\n235\t`docs/WORK_TASKS.md` · `docs/plans/activity-task-operations.md` · ADR-030 ·\n236\t`docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` · `docs/ACTIONS.md`\n237\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"0126eb27-3e97-47c1-bca3-b33338f04892","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:19.054Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018UW4CFvNMzWoA8sGzRGPWT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t// @ts-check\n2\t\n3\timport { AppError, definePackage } from '../../core/index.js';\n4\timport {\n5\t  ACTIVITY_KINDS,\n6\t  ACTIVITY_MODULE,\n7\t  TASK_MODULE,\n8\t  TASK_OPEN,\n9\t  TASK_STATES,\n10\t  TASK_TERMINAL,\n11\t  TASK_TRANSITIONS,\n12\t  bounds,\n13\t  closingKey,\n14\t  createFollowUp,\n15\t  optionalSafeText,\n16\t  recordActivity,\n17\t  requireHumanActor,\n18\t  resolveModule,\n19\t  safeText,\n20\t  sortTimeline,\n21\t} from './follow-up.js';\n22\t\n23\t/**\n24\t * **Work v1 — tasks and activity evidence.**\n25\t *\n26\t * Two records and no more: a **Task** — a unit of human follow-up work arising\n27\t * from a business event — and an **Activity** — an append-only, user-facing\n28\t * entry on a subject's timeline. One capability, `follow-up@1`, lets a\n29\t * consuming domain action create a deterministic follow-up inside its own\n30\t * transaction.\n31\t *\n32\t * **Named `work` deliberately.** `tasks` describes half of it and `activities`\n33\t * the other half; `engagement` would promise a channel that does not exist; and\n34\t * `work-management` would promise planning, scheduling and capacity — which\n35\t * would also collide with Delivery's shipped work packages and milestones.\n36\t * `work` is the smallest noun covering both records, and it is bounded below\n37\t * rather than by its name.\n38\t *\n39\t * **What it deliberately is not, and each is published in `metadata()`:** a\n40\t * scheduler, reminders, calendar sync, email/SMS/WhatsApp, notifications,\n41\t * authenticated or secure assignment, RBAC, a project-management suite, a\n42\t * universal event bus, or a replacement for audit and trace.\n43\t *\n44\t * **Audit versus Activity.** Audit and trace stay the technical record of every\n45\t * write; Activity is the curated semantic one, with a closed four-entry\n46\t * vocabulary. Nothing here projects one into the other: the event bus dispatches\n47\t * after commit (ADR-012), so a projection would either escape the originating\n48\t * transaction or need Jobs/Outbox, which does not exist. Every Activity row is\n49\t * written inside the transaction of the action that caused it.\n50\t */\n51\t\n52\texport const WORK_PACKAGE = 'work';\n53\texport const WORK_RESOURCES = Object.freeze([TASK_MODULE, ACTIVITY_MODULE]);\n54\texport const FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY = Object.freeze({ package: WORK_PACKAGE, capability: 'follow-up', version: 1 });\n55\t\n56\texport {\n57\t  ACTIVITY_KINDS, ACTIVITY_MODULE, TASK_MODULE, TASK_OPEN, TASK_STATES,\n58\t  TASK_TERMINAL, TASK_TRANSITIONS, createFollowUp, sortTimeline,\n59\t};\n60\t\n61\t/** @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames] */\n62\tfunction resolvedNames(moduleNames = {}) {\n63\t  return {\n64\t    task: moduleNames[TASK_MODULE] ?? TASK_MODULE,\n65\t    activity: moduleNames[ACTIVITY_MODULE] ?? ACTIVITY_MODULE,\n66\t  };\n67\t}\n68\t\n69\t/** @param {any} modules @param {string} name */\n70\tfunction activityService(modules, name) {\n71\t  // Through resolveModule: an unregistered name throws out of the registry, so\n72\t  // reading it optionally would let a bare 404 replace this named refusal.\n73\t  const service = resolveModule(modules, name)?.service;\n74\t  if (!service?.createManaged) {\n75\t    throw new AppError(`The work package is installed without its \"${name}\" records`, {\n76\t      code: 'WORK_STORAGE_INVALID', status: 500,\n77\t    });\n78\t  }\n79\t  return service;\n80\t}\n81\t\n82\t/** The subject envelope as it was recorded on the task; never re-resolved. */\n83\tfunction subjectOf(record) {\n84\t  return { resource: record.subjectResource, id: record.subjectId };\n85\t}\n86\t\n87\t/**\n88\t * `complete` — the only way a task becomes `completed`.\n89\t *\n90\t * `fromStates: ['open']` is the transition table, enforced by the action\n91\t * runtime before this code runs: a completed or cancelled task answers `409\n92\t * INVALID_STATE` naming the state it is in. There is no reopen, so both\n93\t * terminal states are genuinely terminal.\n94\t *\n95\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n96\t */\n97\texport function buildCompleteTaskAction(moduleNames) {\n98\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n99\t  return {\n100\t    module: names.task,\n101\t    name: 'complete',\n102\t    label: 'Complete task',\n103\t    description: 'Record that this follow-up work is done. A human decision; it notifies nobody and schedules nothing.',\n104\t    actionContract: 1,\n105\t    stateField: 'status',\n106\t    fromStates: [TASK_OPEN],\n107\t    toState: 'completed',\n108\t    input: [{ name: 'note', type: 'string', required: false, hint: 'Optional: what was done. Recorded on the timeline as text.' }],\n109\t    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, managed, now }) {\n110\t      const completedBy = requireHumanActor(actor, 'Completing a task');\n111\t      const note = optionalSafeText(input.note, 'note', bounds.reason);\n112\t      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n113\t      const completedAt = now();\n114\t      const task = await managed(record.id, {\n115\t        status: 'completed',\n116\t        completedBy,\n117\t        completedAt,\n118\t        closingReason: note,\n119\t      });\n120\t      // Same transaction as the transition: a completed task without its\n121\t      // timeline entry is a pair this package never produces.\n122\t      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n123\t        sourceKey: closingKey(record.id, 'completed'),\n124\t        kind: 'task_completed',\n125\t        subject: subjectOf(record),\n126\t        taskId: record.id,\n127\t        body: note,\n128\t        occurredAt: completedAt,\n129\t        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'complete' },\n130\t      });\n131\t      return { task, activity };\n132\t    },\n133\t  };\n134\t}\n135\t\n136\t/**\n137\t * `cancel` — work that will not be done, with the reason on the record.\n138\t *\n139\t * Cancellation is not completion and the two are never collapsed: a cancelled\n140\t * task is evidence that somebody decided the work was not needed, and reading\n141\t * it as \"done\" would be wrong in the only direction that matters.\n142\t *\n143\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n144\t */\n145\texport function buildCancelTaskAction(moduleNames) {\n146\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n147\t  return {\n148\t    module: names.task,\n149\t    name: 'cancel',\n150\t    label: 'Cancel task',\n151\t    description: 'Record that this follow-up work will not be done, with a reason. Required.' }],\n158\t    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, managed, now }) {\n159\t      const cancelledBy = requireHumanActor(actor, 'Cancelling a task');\n160\t      const reason = safeText(input.reason, 'reason', bounds.reason);\n161\t      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n162\t      const cancelledAt = now();\n163\t      const task = await managed(record.id, {\n164\t        status: 'cancelled',\n165\t        cancelledBy,\n166\t        cancelledAt,\n167\t        closingReason: reason,\n168\t      });\n169\t      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n170\t        sourceKey: closingKey(record.id, 'cancelled'),\n171\t        kind: 'task_cancelled',\n172\t        subject: subjectOf(record),\n173\t        taskId: record.id,\n174\t        body: reason,\n175\t        occurredAt: cancelledAt,\n176\t        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'cancel' },\n177\t      });\n178\t      return { task, activity };\n179\t    },\n180\t  };\n181\t}\n182\t\n183\t/**\n184\t * `add-note` — the one place a human writes free text into the timeline.\n185\t *\n186\t * It has **no `fromStates`**: a note on a closed task is legitimate — often it\n187\t * is the explanation of why it closed the way it did — and refusing it would\n188\t * make the timeline stop exactly where it becomes interesting.\n189\t *\n190\t * It carries **no source key**, deliberately: two identical notes are two\n191\t * statements, not a duplicate, so there is nothing to deduplicate against. That\n192\t * makes a retried note request a second note, which is stated as\n193\t * `MANUAL_NOTES_ARE_NOT_IDEMPOTENT` rather than hidden behind a key that would\n194\t * silently swallow the second one.\n195\t *\n196\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n197\t */\n198\texport function buildAddNoteAction(moduleNames) {\n199\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n200\t  return {\n201\t    module: names.task,\n202\t    name: 'add-note',\n203\t    label: 'Add note',\n204\t    description: 'Append a human note to this task\\'s subject timeline. Safe text only; it is not sent anywhere.',\n205\t    actionContract: 1,\n206\t    input: [{ name: 'body', type: 'string', required: true, hint: 'The note. Plain text, at most 1000 characters.' }],\n207\t    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, now }) {\n208\t      requireHumanActor(actor, 'Adding a note');\n209\t      const body = safeText(input.body, 'body', bounds.noteBody);\n210\t      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n211\t      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n212\t        sourceKey: null,\n213\t        kind: 'note',\n214\t        subject: subjectOf(record),\n215\t        taskId: record.id,\n216\t        body,\n217\t        occurredAt: now(),\n218\t        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'add-note' },\n219\t      });\n220\t      return { activity };\n221\t    },\n222\t  };\n223\t}\n224\t\n225\t/**\n226\t * `follow-up@1` — the declared capability, and the only automatic write path.\n227\t *\n228\t * It is created with the **caller's** runtime handles, so everything it writes\n229\t * happens inside the caller's transaction. It returns a frozen object with two\n230\t * methods and hands out no service, no table, no query handle and no mutable\n231\t * state — there is nothing here a consumer could use to reach around the\n232\t * contract.\n233\t *\n234\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n235\t */\n236\texport function createFollowUpCapability(moduleNames) {\n237\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n238\t  return {\n239\t    name: FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY.capability,\n240\t    version: FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY.version,\n241\t    description:\n242\t      'Create exactly one deterministic follow-up task and its creation activity inside the caller\\'s transaction, keyed by a business identity the caller supplies.',\n243\t    /**\n244\t     * @param {{modules: any, actor?: unknown, now?: () => string, consumer?: string}} context\n245\t     *   `consumer` is added by `PackageRegistry.capability()` from the identity\n246\t     *   it resolved and verified against that package's own declared\n247\t     *   `requires`. It **is** the provenance this capability records: a request\n248\t     *   asserting a different `source.package` is refused 403\n249\t     *   `WORK_SOURCE_PACKAGE_MISMATCH`, so a declared consumer cannot store\n250\t     *   another package's name on work it opened.\n251\t     */\n252\t    create(context) {\n253\t      const scoped = {\n254\t        ...context,\n255\t        modules: {\n256\t          get: (name) => context.modules.get(name === TASK_MODULE ? names.task : name === ACTIVITY_MODULE ? names.activity : name),\n257\t        },\n258\t      };\n259\t      return Object.freeze({\n260\t        /** @param {unknown} request */\n261\t        createFollowUp: (request) => createFollowUp(scoped, request),\n262\t        /**\n263\t         * Read the task a business identity already produced, exactly — never a\n264\t         * paged scan. A consumer that wants to know \"did this already happen?\"\n265\t         * asks this rather than listing.\n266\t         * @param {string} sourceKey\n267\t         */\n268\t        findBySourceKey(sourceKey) {\n269\t          const service = resolveModule(context.modules, names.task)?.service;\n270\t          if (!service?.listWhere) {\n271\t            throw new AppError(`The work package is installed without its \"${names.task}\" records`, {\n272\t              code: 'WORK_STORAGE_INVALID', status: 500,\n273\t            });\n274\t          }\n275\t          return service.listWhere({ sourceKey: String(sourceKey) })[0] ?? null;\n276\t        },\n277\t      });\n278\t    },\n279\t  };\n280\t}\n281\t\n282\t/** @param {{modules?: Record<string, string>}} [options] */\n283\texport function createWorkPackage(options = {}) {\n284\t  return definePackage({\n285\t    packageContract: 1,\n286\t    name: WORK_PACKAGE,\n287\t    version: 1,\n288\t    label: 'Work items and activity evidence',\n289\t    description:\n290\t      'Owns follow-up tasks and an append-only user-facing activity timeline, and offers one capability so a domain action can create a deterministic follow-up inside its own transaction. It schedules nothing, reminds nobody and assigns to nobody.',\n291\t    resources: [...WORK_RESOURCES],\n292\t    // Nothing. Work is consumed, never a consumer, so no composition that adds\n293\t    // it can create a dependency cycle.\n294\t    requires: [],\n295\t    capabilities: [createFollowUpCapability(options.modules)],\n296\t    actions: [\n297\t      buildCompleteTaskAction(options.modules),\n298\t      buildCancelTaskAction(options.modules),\n299\t      buildAddNoteAction(options.modules),\n300\t    ],\n301\t    // None. A versioned policy earns its fingerprint when two consumers share a\n302\t    // rule; these two share none — one wants a lead's name and a due date, the\n303\t    // other a contract intent and no date. See ADR-030.\n304\t    policies: [],\n305\t    metadata() {\n306\t      return {\n307\t        workContract: 1,\n308\t        task: {\n309\t          states: [...TASK_STATES],\n310\t          open: TASK_OPEN,\n311\t          terminal: [...TASK_TERMINAL],\n312\t          transitions: { open: [...TASK_TRANSITIONS.open], completed: [], cancelled: [] },\n313\t          reopen:\n314\t            'not supported in v1. A repeated business round is a new task under a new source key; un-completing work would need a second lifecycle nothing here has',\n315\t          dueAt:\n316\t            'evidence only. Nothing schedules on it, nothing fires from it, and no clock changes a status because of it. A read-only due/overdue may be computed at an injected instant and never writes. It must still name a day that existed: an impossible date is refused rather than rolled over to the next real one (ADR-028)',\n317\t          assignment:\n318\t            'there is no assignee field. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01GZNoBfr5yFN4hBqPaSq7Ho","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Module manifests\n2\t\n3\tA module manifest is a small declarative JSON file that describes one CRM module entity. The framework validates it with precise errors and generates deterministic SQLite migration SQL from it. The same manifest always produces byte-identical output.\n4\t\n5\tManifests do not execute anything and do not touch the database: they are input to explicit, dry-run-by-default CLI commands.\n6\t\n7\t## Example\n8\t\n9\t`examples/modules/partner.module.json`:\n10\t\n11\t```json\n12\t{\n13\t  \"manifestVersion\": 1,\n14\t  \"name\": \"partner\",\n15\t  \"description\": \"Channel partners that resell or refer the product.\",\n16\t  \"fields\": [\n17\t    { \"name\": \"name\", \"type\": \"string\", \"required\": true },\n18\t    { \"name\": \"tier\", \"type\": \"enum\", \"values\": [\"silver\", \"gold\", \"platinum\"] },\n19\t    { \"name\": \"territory\", \"type\": \"string\" }\n20\t  ]\n21\t}\n22\t```\n23\t\n24\t## Manifest schema\n25\t\n26\t| Property | Required | Meaning |\n27\t|---|---|---|\n28\t| `manifestVersion` | no | Manifest format version. Defaults to `1`, the only supported value; a manifest written for a newer format fails with an explicit \"unsupported manifestVersion\" error instead of being misinterpreted. |\n29\t| `name` | yes | Module name, `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$` (example: `partner`, `partner-contract`). |\n30\t| `description` | no | Human/agent-readable description. |\n31\t| `table` | no | Explicit table name (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`). Defaults to the naive plural below. |\n32\t| `fields` | yes | Non-empty array of field objects. |\n33\t\n34\tUnknown properties are rejected so typos fail loudly.\n35\t\n36\t### Fields\n37\t\n38\t| Property | Required | Meaning |\n39\t|---|---|---|\n40\t| `name` | yes | camelCase, `^[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9]*$`. Maps to a snake_case column (`valueCents` → `value_cents`). |\n41\t| `type` | yes | One of `string`, `email`, `integer`, `boolean`, `timestamp`, `enum`, `reference`. |\n42\t| `required` | no | Default `false`. Generates `NOT NULL`. |\n43\t| `unique` | no | Default `false`. Generates `UNIQUE`. |\n44\t| `values` | enum only | Non-empty array of unique strings; generates a `CHECK(col IN (...))`. |\n45\t| `references` | reference only | Target **table** name (example: `\"companies\"`); generates `REFERENCES <table>(id)` and an index on the column. |\n46\t| `onDelete` | reference only | `restrict` (default), `cascade` or `set_null`. `set_null` conflicts with `required: true`. |\n47\t| `writable` | no | `public` (default) or `managed`. A `managed` field is owned by a workflow action: public create/update reject it and it is written only through the service's `applyManaged`. Not allowed on `reference` fields. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`. |\n48\t| `default` | no | Value a `managed` field takes on create. Allowed on `string`/`enum` only; for an `enum` it must be one of `values`; requires `writable: \"managed\"` (public-field defaults are not implemented, so one would silently never apply). |\n49\t\n50\tStructurally unusable managed combinations are rejected at validation instead of generating a module that cannot work: `managed` + `required` needs a `default` (public create cannot supply the value, so every create would violate `NOT NULL`), and `managed` + `unique` cannot carry a `default` (every create would insert the same value; the second would always fail). `module plan` lists each managed field under `managedFields`, so the write policy is visible before applying.\n51\t\n52\t### Explicit conventions (no hidden magic)\n53\t\n54\t- Every generated table gets `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, `created_at TEXT NOT NULL` and `updated_at TEXT NOT NULL` automatically. `id`, `createdAt` and `updatedAt` are therefore reserved field names.\n55\t- Default table name is a documented naive plural of `name`: trailing consonant + `y` → `ies` (`company` → `companies`), trailing `s` → `es` (`business` → `businesses`), otherwise append `s` (`partner` → `partners`). Hyphens become underscores. Use `table` to override.\n56\t- Column types: `integer` and `boolean` → `INTEGER` (`boolean` adds `CHECK(col IN (0, 1))`); everything else → `TEXT`. Tables are `STRICT`.\n57\t- Money is modeled explicitly as an `integer` cents field plus a `string`/`enum` currency field, per repository conventions. There is no composite money type.\n58\t- Field order in the manifest is preserved in the generated SQL.\n59\t- Generated identifiers are unquoted, so table names, column names and `references` targets must not be SQLite keywords (for example `order`, `values`, `select`); validation rejects them with a clear error asking for a rename or an explicit `table`.\n60\t\n61\t## CLI usage\n62\t\n63\t```bash\n64\tnpm run crm -- module validate examples/modules/partner.module.json\n65\tnpm run crm -- module migration examples/modules/partner.module.json --dry-run\n66\tnpm run crm -- module migration examples/modules/partner.module.json --out migrations/create_partners.sql\n67\t```\n68\t\n69\t`module:validate` / `module:migration` and the space-separated aliases are equivalent.\n70\t\n71\t- Migration generation is a **dry-run by default**: it prints `{ mode: \"dry-run\", sql }` and writes nothing.\n72\t- Writing requires an explicit `--out <file>`; an existing file is never overwritten unless `--force` is passed.\n73\t- An explicit `--dry-run` flag always wins over `--out`.\n74\t- Validation failures exit non-zero and list every problem, naming the offending field.\n75\t\n76\t## How coding agents should use this\n77\t\n78\t1. Write the manifest under `examples/modules/` or the module's own directory.\n79\t2. Run `module validate` and fix every reported error.\n80\t3. For a complete runnable module (service, migration, registration, tests), use the **module factory**: `module plan` then `module create --apply` — see `docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md`. No manual `MIGRATIONS` or `create-app.js` edit is needed.\n81\t4. For hand-written services (e.g. modules with reference fields), run `module migration --dry-run`, review the SQL, and add it as a migration yourself.\n82\t5. Business logic stays explicit code — manifests generate infrastructure. Mutations still go through module services and workflows with validation, audit and trace.\n83\t6. Finish with `npm run verify`.\n84\t\n85\t## Reference fields (Milestone 5)\n86\t\n87\tA `reference` field links a record to another generated record (many-to-one). `references` names the **target table**; the framework derives the target module from installed-module metadata.\n88\t\n89\t```json\n90\t{ \"name\": \"partnerId\", \"type\": \"reference\", \"references\": \"partners\", \"required\": true }\n91\t```\n92\t\n93\t- The target generated module must already be applied; `module plan`/`create` reports each reference's `targetModule`/`targetTable` and rejects a missing target (\"apply the target module first\").\n94\t- `required` → non-null foreign key; optional → nullable, and an optional reference is cleared by submitting `null`.\n95\t- Generated-to-**core** references (e.g. `companies`) are rejected in this milestone.\n96\t- Optional self-references are supported; required self-references are rejected at plan time; cross-module cycles are not constructible via the CLI (see below).\n97\t- A missing target at create/update is a `VALIDATION_ERROR` tied to the field — no write, audit or event. The SQLite foreign key (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`) enforces integrity as defense in depth. See `docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md` and ADR-010.\n98\t\n99\t### Reference self-references and cycles\n100\t\n101\t- An **optional** self-reference (`references` = the module's own table, not `required`) is supported: the first record leaves it null, then may point at any existing record including itself.\n102\t- A **required** self-reference is rejected at plan time — the first record would be impossible to create.\n103\t- Cross-module cycles are not constructible through the CLI (a reference's target must be applied first), so they are neither generated nor claimed as supported.\n104\t\n105\t## Indexes and read-only modules (Milestone 9, ADR-015)\n106\t\n107\t- A field may declare `\"index\": true` to generate a plain secondary index for\n108\t  exact-match correctness queries (`listWhere`/`countWhere` on the generated\n109\t  service). It is rejected as redundant on `unique` fields (the constraint is\n110\t  already indexed) and on `reference` fields (indexed automatically).\n111\t- A module whose **every** field is `\"writable\": \"managed\"` is generated as a\n112\t  **read-only public module**: capabilities `[\"get\", \"list\"]`, no public\n113\t  `create`/`update` at all (HTTP POST/PATCH fail closed; the Admin shows no\n114\t  Create/Edit), and a trusted in-process `createManaged(patch, ctx)` alongside\n115\t  `applyManaged` for action code. Used by the Lead Intelligence record modules\n116\t  (snapshots, signals, runs, contributions, evaluations, assignments).\n117\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"8dcd27b4-95e2-4902-aa15-63d4f5a5ef57","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:34.525Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}

After an explicit apply, no manual step remains: the next `createAccordoApp` start migrates and serves the module.\n4\t\n5\t## Commands\n6\t\n7\t```bash\n8\tnpm run crm -- module plan examples/modules/partner.module.json          # read-only plan\n9\tnpm run crm -- module plan examples/modules/partner.module.json --json   # same (output is always JSON)\n10\t\n11\tnpm run crm -- module create examples/modules/partner.module.json          # dry-run\n12\tnpm run crm -- module create examples/modules/partner.module.json --apply  # writes\n13\t```\n14\t\n15\t- `module plan` is **always read-only**. It reports, for every file the apply would touch: relative path, action (`create`/`modify`), whether it already exists, byte size, `contentSha256` and a preview. The dry-run of `module create` returns the **same plan** that `--apply` executes.\n16\t- `module create` with a `.json` argument runs the factory; with a bare name it keeps the legacy template scaffold. Both are dry-run by default; `--apply` is required to write.\n17\t- Invalid manifests exit non-zero listing every problem; re-applying an existing module is refused (`refusing to overwrite`) — files are never silently overwritten.\n18\t\n19\t## What gets generated (for `partner`)\n20\t\n21\t| Path | Purpose |\n22\t|---|---|\n23\t| `packages/modules/partner/module.manifest.json` | normalized manifest — the module's source of truth |\n24\t| `packages/modules/partner/src/migration.js` | `{ name: 'create_partners', sql }` |\n25\t| `packages/modules/partner/src/partner-service.js` | create / get / list (limit-capped) / update, manifest-derived validation, UNIQUE→ConflictError, audit + domain events on every mutation |\n26\t| `packages/modules/partner/src/index.js` | module definition + `createPartnerModule(deps)` |\n27\t| `tests/partner-module.test.js` | registration + CRUD + audit test against `:memory:` |\n28\t| `packages/modules/generated/index.js` (modified) | regenerated registry, sorted by module name |\n29\t\n30\tGenerated files carry a header stating their origin; they are **yours to edit** — the factory never rewrites an existing module.\n31\t\n32\t## Semantics of generated services\n33\t\n34\t- **Unknown input properties are ignored** on create and update: only manifest-declared fields are read, validated and persisted (the same policy as the handwritten core modules).\n35\t- **Mutations are atomic with their audit record**: the data write and `audit.record` run inside one SAVEPOINT (safe even inside an enclosing workflow transaction); if either fails, neither persists and **no domain event is emitted**. Events fire only after the savepoint is released.\n36\t- `list` accepts only integer limits (anything else falls back to the default 100, capped at 500) and orders by `created_at DESC, id` so pagination is deterministic.\n37\t- `update` changes only the supplied fields; `id`, `createdAt` and `updatedAt` are never client-writable; an empty update is a no-op returning the current record; a missing id raises `NotFoundError`.\n38\t- **Managed fields are never client-writable.** For a field declared `\"writable\": \"managed\"`, `create` takes the declared `default` (never input), `update` ignores it, and either one **rejects** it with a field-tied `VALIDATION_ERROR` if it appears in the input. The generated service instead exposes `applyManaged(id, patch, ctx)` — the single privileged path that validates and writes managed fields with audit and event, used by record actions through `ctx.managed` and never routed over HTTP. Passing `null` clears a managed field. See `docs/ACTIONS.md` and ADR-011.\n39\t\n40\t## How registration works\n41\t\n42\t`packages/modules/generated/index.js` is a checked-in registry with static imports, regenerated on every apply from the `module.manifest.json` copies found under `packages/modules/*/` (each one re-validated during the scan — a malformed manifest fails the plan loudly instead of producing a registry that breaks app boot). `createAccordoApp` imports it, passes each module's migration to the database layer and registers each factory. The application stays synchronous; there is no dynamic loading or runtime eval. See ADR-007.\n43\t\n44\t**Collision policy** (checked at plan time, before anything is written): module names may not be `generated`, may not match a core/handwritten module (case-insensitively), and generated tables may not claim a core framework table or another generated module's table.\n45\t\n46\t**Migration identity and drift**: each module migration is identified by name and a SHA-256 checksum of its SQL, recorded in `module_migrations` when applied. Re-applying the same name+SQL is a no-op; the same name with **changed SQL fails loudly** (\"applied migrations are immutable — add a new migration instead of editing an applied one\"); duplicate names across modules are rejected; a failed migration is rolled back and never recorded. Automatic schema alteration is out of scope for this milestone.\n47\t\n48\t## Field support\n49\t\n50\t`string`, `email`, `integer`, `boolean`, `timestamp`, `enum` (+ `required`/`unique`, and `writable`/`default` for workflow-managed fields) generate service validation matching the core modules' idiom. **`reference` fields** (many-to-one to another generated module) are supported since Milestone 5 — see \"Reference fields\" below and ADR-010. Generated-to-core references remain rejected until an explicit adapter exists.\n51\t\n52\t## Safety and determinism\n53\t\n54\t- Byte-identical output for the same manifest and framework version; no timestamps, randomness or machine paths in generated files (`\\n` endings).\n55\t- Apply is atomic: files are staged to temp names and renamed together; any failure rolls back created files, created directories and the registry.\n56\t- Paths are confined to the project root; module names cannot traverse.\n57\t- SQLite identifier/keyword/literal safety inherited from manifest validation.\n58\t\n59\t## For coding agents (Claude Code / Codex)\n60\t\n61\t1. `module validate` → fix every reported problem.\n62\t2. `module plan` → review the file list and hashes.\n63\t3. `module create` (dry-run) → confirm the plan matches intent.\n64\t4. `module create --apply` → then `npm run verify` (the generated test runs with the suite).\n65\t5. Add domain rules by editing the generated service — keep audit and events on every mutation; cross-module processes belong in workflows, not in the service.\n66\t\n67\t## From apply to API and SDK (Milestone 3)\n68\t\n69\tAfter `--apply`, the module is automatically served over HTTP and usable from the SDK — no route or SDK file to write:\n70\t\n71\t```bash\n72\tnpm run crm -- module create examples/modules/partner.module.json --apply\n73\tnpm run dev            # start the server\n74\t```\n75\t\n76\t```js\n77\timport { AccordoClient } from './packages/sdk/src/index.js';\n78\t\n79\tconst client = new AccordoClient({\n80\t  baseUrl: 'http://localhost:4000',\n81\t  actor: { type: 'agent', id: 'claude-code' },\n82\t});\n83\t\n84\tconst schema = await client.schema();          // generatedModules lists partner + fields\n85\tconst partners = client.module('partner');     // frozen resource client\n86\t\n87\tawait partners.metadata();                     // fields, capabilities, paths\n88\tconst created = await partners.create({ name: 'Acme Partners', tier: 'gold', territory: 'Italy' });\n89\tawait partners.get(created.id);\n90\tawait partners.list({ limit: 50 });\n91\tawait partners.update(created.id, { tier: 'platinum' });\n92\t```\n93\t\n94\tDiscovery for agents: `GET /api/schema` → `generatedModules[]` carries every field's type/required/unique/enum values plus the canonical paths — no need to read internal database code. See `docs/API.md` for the full contract. The server stays local-development-only until auth/tenancy/roles exist.\n95\t\n96\t## Reference fields (Milestone 5)\n97\t\n98\tA `reference` field (`{\"type\":\"reference\",\"references\":\"<target-table>\"}`) generates a real SQLite foreign key plus **runtime** target validation through the application reference resolver (ADR-010) — not a cross-module SQL query, not a static import between modules.\n99\t\n100\t```bash\n101\tnpm run crm -- module create examples/modules/partner.module.json --apply          # target first\n102\tnpm run crm -- module plan   examples/modules/partner-contact.module.json           # shows the reference dependency\n103\tnpm run crm -- module create examples/modules/partner-contact.module.json --apply\n104\t```\n105\t\n106\t- The generated service validates each reference via `this.references.assertTarget(targetTable, value, field)` before the mutation savepoint: a missing/blank required target, or an id that does not exist, throws a field `ValidationError` with no write/audit/event.\n107\t- Optional references: omit to leave unset, send `null` to clear.\n108\t- The target module must be applied first; core-table and unknown targets are rejected at plan time.\n109\t- In the Admin, a reference renders as a target selector labelled by the target's display field, with the current value always preserved (fetched by id if not on the first page). Large target sets will need a searchable control in a later milestone.\n110\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"1ce8850c-48fc-49db-ba0d-9f0d1b6b21e1","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:39.732Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FCEXCwRMNNnNk64sD8SWUL","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Architecture\n2\t\n3\t## Core rule\n4\t\n5\t> CRM state is deterministic. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n6\t\n7\t## Medusa-inspired mapping\n8\t\n9\t| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n10\t|---|---|\n11\t| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n12\t| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n13\t| Workflow | Commercial process |\n14\t| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n15\t| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n16\t| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n17\t| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n18\t| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n19\t\n20\t## Layers\n21\t\n22\t```text\n23\tAdmin / API / SDK\n24\t        ↓\n25\tWorkflows and policies\n26\t        ↓\n27\tCRM module services\n28\t        ↓\n29\tSQLite adapter\n30\t\n31\tMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\n32\tTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n33\t```\n34\t\n35\t## Modules\n36\t\n37\tA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n38\t\n39\tCurrent modules:\n40\t\n41\t- Company\n42\t- Contact\n43\t- Opportunity\n44\t- Approval\n45\t\n46\t## Workflows\n47\t\n48\tA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n49\t\n50\tCurrent workflows:\n51\t\n52\t- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n53\t- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n54\t\n55\t## Actions\n56\t\n57\tA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n58\t\n59\tThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n60\t\n61\tUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n62\t\n63\t## Domain packages\n64\t\n65\tDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n66\t\n67\t`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n68\t\n69\t## Providers\n70\t\n71\tProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n72\t\n73\t## MCP responsibilities\n74\t\n75\tMCP exposes:\n76\t\n77\t- project and CRM context as resources;\n78\t- controlled application operations as tools;\n79\t- reusable build/debug prompts.\n80\t\n81\tIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n82\t\n83\t## Extension rule\n84\t\n85\tA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n86\t\n87\t1. one module;\n88\t2. one workflow;\n89\t3. one API or MCP surface;\n90\t4. tests;\n91\t5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n92\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"28793587-6fe3-4020-be35-eef4d0dda834","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:45.464Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}

No domain is refactored by the PR that\n4\tintroduced this document, and none should be until the sequencing below allows\n5\tit.**\n6\t\n7\tTwo domains — Contract Activation and Delivery — were built *after* the domain\n8\tpackage seam (ADR-018) and use it. Three older ones — Lead Intelligence,\n9\tCommercial Operations, and Signature & Order — were built before it and lived in\n10\t`packages/core/src/`. That was a fact, not a defect: they were built when the\n11\tseam did not exist, and each one is what taught us what the seam had to be.\n12\t\n13\t**Lead Intelligence has since been extracted** and is now a package like any\n14\tother. It is the proof that a legacy domain can move without changing what it\n15\tdecides: LA0 froze its externally observable behaviour first, and zero asserted\n16\tobservations moved across the extraction. Commercial Operations and Signature &\n17\tOrder are still where they were.\n18\t\n19\tPipeline is in `packages/core/src/` too and belongs there. It is a reusable\n20\truntime capability rather than a domain, and it is in the matrix so that a reader\n21\tscanning core for misplaced business logic is told why it is not one.\n22\t\n23\tThe risk is not that they are old. The risk is **drift by silence**: a horizontal\n24\tcapability lands, the newest domain gets it, the older ones are never assessed,\n25\tand after four milestones nobody can say which domain has what. This document\n26\texists so that never has to be reconstructed from source.\n27\t\n28\t## Status vocabulary, used exactly\n29\t\n30\t| Status | Means |\n31\t|---|---|\n32\t| `aligned` | the domain uses the capability as the contract intends |\n33\t| `partial` | it uses part of it, or uses an equivalent that is not the contract |\n34\t| `deferred` | it should use it, it does not, and the milestone that closes the gap is named |\n35\t| `not_applicable` | the capability does not apply to this domain, with the reason |\n36\t| `needs_extraction` | the gap cannot close while the domain lives in `packages/core` |\n37\t\n38\t`needs_extraction` is the one that matters. It says the blocker is **structural**,\n39\tnot effort — no amount of care inside `packages/core/src/commercial-actions.js`\n40\tmakes Commercial a package.\n41\t\n42\t## Where each domain lives today\n43\t\n44\tVerified against the working tree, 2026-08-07.\n45\t\n46\t| Domain | Runtime home | Is it a package? |\n47\t|---|---|---|\n48\t| Core CRM (Sales) | project-generated modules: company, contact, opportunity, lead, task, approval | no — these are a project's own records, not a domain package |\n49\t| Pipeline | `packages/core/src/pipeline-*.js`; `packages/pipelines/generated/` | no — and correctly so, see ¹ |\n50\t| Lead Intelligence | `packages/intelligence/` — `src/`, `modules/`, `README.md` | **yes** — the first legacy domain extracted |\n51\t| Commercial Operations | `packages/core/src/commercial-*.js`, `catalog-sync.js`; `packages/commercial/generated/` | no |\n52\t| Signature & Order | `packages/core/src/signature-*.js`, `external-operation.js`; `packages/signature/generated/` | no |\n53\t| Contract Activation | `packages/contracts/` — `src/`, `modules/`, `README.md` | **yes** |\n54\t| Delivery | `packages/delivery/` — `src/`, `modules/`, `README.md` | **yes** |\n55\t| Custom-package fixture | `examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/` | **yes** — the customer-authoring proof |\n56\t| Service | not built (M15) | — |\n57\t| Marketing & Growth | documentation only (`docs/strategy/`) | — |\n58\t\n59\tEach of the four has a `packages/<name>/generated/` directory and nothing else:\n60\tthat directory is where a composed project registers the domain's policies and\n61\tproviders, not where the domain lives.\n62\t\n63\t## The matrix\n64\t\n65\tColumns are the six built domains. Read a row as: *does this domain use this\n66\thorizontal capability the way the contract intends?*\n67\t\n68\t### Hosted Docs MCP transport assessment\n69\t\n70\tThe stateless Streamable HTTP transport in `packages/docs-mcp/src/http.js` is a\n71\thorizontal **documentation/distribution** surface, not a CRM runtime capability.\n72\tIts status is `not_applicable` for Pipeline, Lead Intelligence, Commercial\n73\tOperations, Signature & Order, Contract Activation and Delivery: it reads the\n74\trepository-wide public documentation corpus and claims ledger, imports no domain\n75\tpackage, opens no application/database and exposes no domain mutation. No legacy\n76\tdomain can align to it or be backfilled into it. This explicit assessment closes\n77\tthe Compatibility Backfill Rule for the transport without inventing six empty\n78\truntime integrations.\n79\t\n80\t| Horizontal capability | Pipeline | Lead
