| `aligned` | `aligned` |\n98\t| **AX1 visibility** — appears in `app inspect` as a package with resources, actions and capabilities | `partial` ¹ — its actions are reported; there is no package to report | `aligned` — discovered as a package, with no fixed slot | `partial` | `partial` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n99\t| **AX2 citability** — a Solution Plan can cite the domain's capabilities and record revisions | `partial` ¹ | `aligned` — `intelligence@1` is citable | `partial` | `partial` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n100\t| **Package-scoped Admin section** — renders only while the package's schema metadata is published | `not_applicable` — the board is a core Admin feature | `not_applicable` | `partial` — the quote screens are core Admin, not gated on package metadata | `partial` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n101\t| **Detach/reattach proof** — removing the domain removes its whole surface and nothing else | `not_applicable` ¹ | `aligned` | `needs_extraction` | `needs_extraction` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n102\t| **Fault-injection and two-connection evidence** | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n103\t| **Migration array with per-entry checksums** — an applied migration cannot be edited | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n104\t| **Declared action metadata** — `fromStates`/`toState` published in the schema and in `app inspect` | `aligned` | `not_applicable` — scoring and routing are not transitions | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n105\t| **A domain Skill, mirrored in `.claude/` and `.agents/`** | `partial` — covered by `create-crm-workflow`, no pipeline-specific Skill | `partial` ² | `partial` ² | `partial` ² | `partial` ² | `partial` ² |\n106\t| **A tool namespace of its own** | `not_applicable` | `deferred` — DX13 | `deferred` — DX13 | `deferred` — DX13 | `deferred` — DX13 | `deferred` — DX13 |\n107\t| **JTBD rows with linked evidence** | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` | `aligned` |\n108\t| **Scenario evidence (DX6)** — a checked-in scenario runs a real journey and maps what it observed onto the domain's JTBD rows, with the rows it did *not* establish stated | `aligned` — JTBD-03 claimed and established | `aligned` — LI-01, LI-02, LI-04 claimed and established; LI-07 is not claimed | `aligned` — CO-01, CO-03, CO-07 | `partial` ³ | `partial` ³ | `partial` ³ |\n109\t\n110\t### The domains outside the six-column table\n111\t\n112\t| Domain | Where it stands |\n113\t|---|---|\n114\t| **Core CRM (Sales)** — company, contact, opportunity, lead, task, approval | `not_applicable` on every package-seam row: these are a *project's* generated records, not a domain package, and a customer's own CRM objects must never require one. `aligned` on the kernel rows (module evolution, migration checksums, managed fields where declared, events, audit and trace, exact reads, JTBD evidence). Its Skills are `create-crm-module` and `create-crm-workflow` |\n115\t| **Custom-package fixture** (`examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/`) | `aligned` on the package seam by construction — it exists to prove a customer-authored package attaches, works and detaches with no kernel change. It deliberately exercises only a slice: one resource, one action, no capability of its own, so the capability rows read `not_applicable` |\n116\t| **Service** | **built and merged (M15).** Package-native from its first commit: `aligned` on the package seam, declared capabilities (requires `contracts/service-obligations@1`, provides three), `packageContract: 1` conformance, package version discipline, managed records, the human-actor boundary, fingerprinted declared definitions, transaction-scoped events, audit and trace, exact reads, AX1 visibility, AX2 citability, detach proof, fault-injection and two-connection evidence, and JTBD rows with linked evidence. `not_applicable` on the money contract and the external-operation contract: it prices nothing and calls no provider. `partial` ² on the Skill mirror — the mirrors currently agree, but nothing keeps them agreeing; the same DX2 gap every domain has. `deferred` on a tool namespace: §C.2b of `AGENT_TOOL_SURFACE.md` now works one through on Service, and it stays a proposal until DX13 |\n117\t| **Marketing & Growth** | documentation only. No row can be assessed, and none is claimed |\n118\t\n119\t**Scenario evidence for the domains outside the six-column table.** Core CRM\n120\t(Sales) is `aligned` — JTBD-04, JTBD-05 and JTBD-05b are claimed and established\n121\tby the `lead-to-won` scenario. The custom-package fixture is `aligned` —\n122\tJTBD-PK-01 and JTBD-PK-02. **Service is now `aligned`**: it was `deferred`\n123\tbecause its package composed into the journey's application while nothing\n124\tobserved it, and `examples/scenarios/service-sla-escalation.scenario.json` closes\n125\tthat — JTBD-DS-11 and JTBD-DS-12 are claimed and established over a **second**\n126\tjourney with an injected clock, and both rows stay *partially supported* because\n127\ta run promotes nothing. Closing it needed no milestone, but it did need one code\n128\tchange nobody predicted: the observation vocabulary had no way to state a\n129\t*non-numeric outcome*, which is what a support process is judged on. Marketing &\n130\tGrowth is `not_applicable` — documentation only.\n131\t\n132\t³ **`partial` on scenario evidence** means the shipped scenario reaches the\n133\tdomain and claims part of it, not all of it. The journey drives the signature\n134\tenvelope, webhook verification and reconciliation, the subscription activation,\n135\tand delivery handover — but a claim only exists where a scenario wrote one, and\n136\tDX6 publishes that as `COVERAGE_IS_CLAIMED_NOT_DISCOVERED`. Delivery execution,\n137\teconomics, change and acceptance (M14a/M14b1/M14b2) are not in this journey at\n138\tall. Closing these is writing scenarios, not changing any domain.\n139\t\n140\t¹ **Pipeline is not a domain.** `buildMoveStageAction` is a generic factory that\n141\tstages *any* module a project points it at — a reusable runtime capability, which\n142\tis exactly what ADR-018's core budget rule permits in `packages/core`. Extracting\n143\tit would be a mistake, not a backfill. It appears in this matrix because a reader\n144\tscanning for \"everything in core\" will find it, and needs to be told why it is\n145\tthere.\n146\t\n147\t³ **The refusal receipt is a site content contract, not a domain runtime\n148\tcapability.** Pipeline, Lead Intelligence, Commercial Operations, Signature &\n149\tOrder, Contract Activation and Delivery are therefore `not_applicable`; so are\n150\tCore CRM, the custom-package fixture, Service and documentation-only Marketing &\n151\tGrowth. Their actual human-actor behavior remains assessed by the row immediately\n152\tabove. The Smart CRM page uses Commercial's tested quote refusal as evidence, but\n153\tthat does not make the renderer a capability Commercial must adopt or other domains\n154\tmust backfill.\n155\t\n156\t⁴ **The responsibility map is also a site content contract, not a domain runtime\n157\tcapability.** Every domain is `not_applicable`, including those outside the six-column\n158\ttable. The CDP + CRM page cites Lead Intelligence and the general mutation envelope as\n159\tevidence for the process layer; it does not add a CDP dependency, integration seam or\n160\tnew obligation to any domain package.\n161\t\n162\t⁵ **The recommendation measurement identity contract is a project-level GTM\n163\tevidence discipline, not a domain runtime capability.** Every domain is\n164\t`not_applicable`, including Core CRM, the custom-package fixture, Service and\n165\tdocumentation-only Marketing & Growth. It governs whether an external model response\n166\tmay enter a public metric; it adds no requirement to a package, record, action, policy\n167\tor provider.\n168\t\n169\t### Reading the shape rather than the cells\n170\t\n171\tThe rows split cleanly into two groups, and the split is the whole finding.\n172\t\n173\t**Rows where every domain is `aligned`** — Module Evolution, transaction-scoped\n174\tevents, audit and trace, fault-injection and two-connection evidence, JTBD\n175\tevidence. These are **kernel capabilities**: a domain gets them by existing.\n176\tThey needed no backfill because there was never a version of the framework where\n177\tone domain had them and another did not. That is the argument for putting a\n178\thorizontal capability in the kernel when it honestly is one — and ADR-018's core\n179\tbudget rule is the argument against doing it when it is not.\n180\t\n181\t**Rows where the three older domains are `needs_extraction`** — the package seam,\n182\tdeclared capabilities, contract conformance, detach proof. Every one of them is\n183\tthe *same* gap wearing a different name. There are not four problems here; there\n184\tis one, and extraction is its only fix. The `partial` AX1 and AX2 rows are that\n185\tsame gap seen from the agent's side: an agent can cite an action of theirs, but\n186\tthere is no package or capability to cite.\n187\t\n188\tEverything else is `partial` or `not_applicable`, and `partial` is where an\n189\targument is worth having — `not_applicable` rows are settled by the domain's\n190\tnature.\n191\t\n192\t## Every non-aligned cell, in full\n193\t\n194\tThe matrix gives a status. A status without a consequence is a colour. Each\n195\tentry below states the **gap**, the **evidence** it rests on, the **pass that\n196\tcloses it**, and the **compatibility risk** of closing it.\n197\t\n198\t### `needs_extraction` — Lead Intelligence, Commercial Operations, Signature & Order\n199\t\n200\tFour rows, one gap: the package seam, declared capabilities, `packageContract`\n201\tconformance, and the detach proof.\n202\t\n203\t- **Gap.** The domain's runtime lives in `packages/core/src/`, so it declares no\n204\t  package, owns no resources through a package, publishes no capability and\n205\t  cannot be detached.\n206\t- **Evidence.** `packages/{intelligence,commercial,signature}/` contain only\n207\t  `generated/`; the runtime is `packages/core/src/{intelligence,commercial,signature}-*.js`.\n208\t  `app inspect` on a composed project lists their actions and modules and **no\n209\t  package entry** for them.\n210\t- **Pass that closes it.** The controlled Legacy Domain Alignment Pass, after\n211\t  M15 and after DX4 makes conformance mechanical. One domain, one PR.\n212\t- **Compatibility risk.** **High, and it is data risk, not code risk.** These\n213\t  domains own scoring runs, quote versions, signed orders and provider\n214\t  definitions whose fingerprints are checked at startup. A move must preserve\n215\t  every source key, every stored fingerprint and every historical decision\n216\t  byte-for-byte. The acceptance criterion is behavior preservation proved from\n217\t  outside — a \"cleaner\" extraction that changes one recorded outcome has failed.\n218\t\n219\t### `partial` — AX1 visibility and AX2 citability, same three domains\n220\t\n221\t- **Gap.** An agent can cite an action or a record revision, but there is no\n222\t  package version or capability to cite, so a Solution Plan cannot bind to them\n223\t  the way it binds to `delivery@4`.\n224\t- **Evidence.** `packages: []` for them in `app inspect`; the AX2 example plan\n225\t  can only pin `contracts` and `delivery`.\n226\t- **Pass.** Closes automatically with extraction — it is the same gap seen from\n227\t  the agent's side, not separate work.\n228\t- **Compatibility risk.** Low on its own; it inherits the extraction's risk.\n229\t\n230\t### `partial` ² — the Skill mirrors: the state is clean, the mechanism is not\n231\t\n232\t**Re-measured against the current tree, because the earlier entry was stale.**\n233\tIt said the domain build Skills existed under `.claude/skills/` only and that\n234\t`.agents/skills/` carried none of them. That was true when it was written and is\n235\t**no longer true**: the agent-discovery work merged to main copied the mirrors\n236\tacross.\n237\t\n238\t- **Measured now.** `.claude/skills/` and `.agents/skills/` each carry **12**\n239\t  skills, with **none** on one side only. `accordo project doctor` reports\n240\t  `skills.mirror-coverage` **passing** and `skills.mirror-drift` **passing**,\n241\t  and the whole report is `passed`.\n242\t- **So why is this still `partial`?** Because the row is about a *capability\n243\t  used as the contract intends*, and the contract needs the mirrors to **stay**\n244\t  in agreement. Nothing keeps them there. There is no canonical source, no\n245\t  `sync`, no CI drift gate and no adapter generation — Project Doctor *detects*\n246\t  disagreement and, by design, never writes. The debt was paid down **by hand,\n247\t  in state**; the **mechanism** is still missing, and the next skill added to\n248\t  one side re-opens it silently until somebody runs the doctor.\n249\t- **Evidence.** `ls .claude/skills/` versus `ls .agents/skills/`;\n250\t  `accordo project doctor --json` → `skills.mirror-coverage`, `skills.mirror-drift`.\n251\t- **Pass.** **DX2** (`crm agent skills sync|check`) — unchanged, and now the\n252\t  *only* thing missing rather than one of two.\n253\t- **Compatibility risk.** **None.** Additive files; no runtime reads them.\n254\t\n255\tThis is the distinction the matrix exists to keep: a green check today is not an\n256\taligned capability. Marking this row `aligned` on the strength of a clean `ls`\n257\tis exactly the drift-by-silence the document was written to prevent.\n258\t\n259\t### `partial` — Commercial Operations against the external-operation contract\n260\t\n261\t- **Gap.** Catalog sync predates ADR-017 and uses its own\n262\t  fetch-outside-the-transaction then reconcile-inside-one shape rather than the\n263\t  intent / call / finalize / compensate contract Signature established.\n264\t- **Evidence.** `packages/core/src/catalog-sync.js` versus\n265\t  `packages/core/src/external-operation.js`.\n266\t- **Pass.** Deferred, with no milestone named — and deliberately so. Catalog sync\n267\t  is idempotent by DB-unique source keys and has its own tests; rewriting a\n268\t  working external boundary to match a later contract is a change with real risk\n269\t  and no user-visible benefit.\n270\t- **Compatibility risk.** **Medium.** It touches a provider boundary and a\n271\t  reconciliation path that customers' catalogs depend on.\n272\t\n273\t### `partial` — the Admin sections of Commercial Operations and Signature & Order\n274\t\n275\t- **Gap.** Their screens are core Admin, not gated on package metadata, so they\n276\t  do not disappear when a package does.\n277\t- **Evidence.** `apps/admin/public/admin-quotes.js` and `admin-signature.js`\n278\t  render unconditionally; `admin-delivery-change.js` returns early without\n279\t  `schema.domains.delivery.changeAcceptance`.\n280\t- **Pass.** Follows extraction; there is nothing to gate on until a package\n281\t  publishes metadata.\n282\t- **Compatibility risk.** Low.\n283\t\n284\t### `partial` — Pipeline's managed records, human boundary and fingerprints\n285\t\n286\t- **Gap.** A stage is current state rather than append-only evidence; the human\n287\t  boundary lives in the approval workflow around a staged move rather than in\n288\t  `move-stage`; a pipeline definition is validated but carries no\n289\t  content-addressed version.\n290\t- **Evidence.** `packages/core/src/pipeline-actions.js` and\n291\t  `pipeline-registry.js`; the human check is in\n292\t  `packages/workflows/src/decide-opportunity-approval.js`.\n293\t- **Pass.** **None planned, and none needed.** These are properties of a generic\n294\t  staging mechanism, not gaps in a domain. Recording them stops a future reader\n295\t  concluding Pipeline was overlooked.\n296\t- **Compatibility risk.** Not applicable.\n297\t\n298\t### `deferred` — a tool namespace, every domain\n299\t\n300\t- **Gap.** No domain has an MCP namespace; the shipped MCP server exposes a\n301\t  sample domain that predates AX1 and AX2.\n302\t- **Evidence.** `docs/MCP.md`; `docs/architecture/AGENT_TOOL_SURFACE.md` §B.3.\n303\t- **Pass.** **DX13**, and only after the exposure policy in that document is a\n304\t  decision rather than a proposal.\n305\t- **Compatibility risk.** Low — additive — but it is the row most likely to be\n306\t  closed badly. §C.0 exists to stop \"one tool per package\" being the answer.\n307\t\n308\t## The Compatibility Backfill Rule\n309\t\n310\t> When a PR introduces or changes a **horizontal** capability — one that every\n311\t> domain could use — it must, in that same PR, record every existing domain's\n312\t> status against it in this matrix, using the vocabulary above, with a one-line\n313\t> reason. A `deferred` status must name the milestone that closes it.\n314\t>\n315\t> **Declaring the gap is mandatory. Closing it in the same PR is not.**\n316\t> Retrofitting five domains inside a feature PR is how a feature PR stops being\n317\t> reviewable. But a capability that only the newest domain has, and that nobody\n318\t> wrote down, is a fork rather than a platform.\n319\t\n320\tFive questions, answered in the PR body:\n321\t\n322\t```text\n323\tWhich old domains does this touch?\n324\tWhich are already aligned?\n325\tWhich need metadata only?\n326\tWhich need a code backfill?\n327\tWas the Legacy Alignment Matrix updated?\n328\t```\n329\t\n330\tThe distinction between the third and fourth is the useful one. A metadata gap —\n331\ta manifest flag, a published field, a Skill file — is cheap and can often be\n332\tclosed immediately. A code backfill is a change to a domain's runtime, and for a\n333\t`needs_extraction` domain it is not closeable at all until that domain moves. A\n334\tPR that cannot tell the two apart has not looked.\n335\t\n336\tThree practical consequences:\n337\t\n338\t1. The row is written by the PR that creates the capability, not by whoever\n339\t   later notices the gap.\n340\t2. `needs_extraction` is an acceptable answer. It is not an admission of failure;\n341\t   it is the honest name for a structural blocker.\n342\t3. A reviewer may reject a PR for a missing row the same way they reject a\n343\t   missing test — and `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1 now says so.\n344\t\n345\t## The Work v1 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n346\t\n347\tWork v1 (ADR-030) introduces a **horizontal** capability: `work/follow-up@1`, a\n348\tshared Task and Activity model any domain could consume, plus the *opaque subject\n349\tenvelope* those records carry. Every domain could open a follow-up, so every\n350\tdomain gets a row — and the honest answer for most of them is that they should\n351\tnot.\n352\t\n353\t| Question | Answer |\n354\t|---|---|\n355\t| Which old domains does this touch? | Potentially all of them. In practice three business events actually imply human work today: Lead qualification (host), Lifecycle's commercial follow-up, Service escalation. |\n356\t| Which are already aligned? | **Core CRM (Lead)** through the host path, **Lifecycle** and **Service** through the declared capability — all three opt in explicitly |\n357\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n358\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None, and that is the finding.** A domain does not become non-conforming by *not* opening a task. Creating a follow-up where the business event does not imply human work would be worse than the gap: it would put rows in somebody's queue that nobody asked for. Contract Activation, Delivery, Commercial Ops, Signature & Order, Pipeline and Lead Intelligence are `not_applicable` for exactly that reason, each stated below |\n359\t| Was the matrix updated? | Yes — the two rows below, this section, and the three domains outside the six-column table |\n360\t\n361\t| Horizontal capability | Pipeline | Lead Intelligence | Commercial Ops | Signature & Order | Contract Activation | Delivery |\n362\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n363\t| **Shared follow-up capability** (`work/follow-up@1`) — human work arising from a business event is one model, not a table per domain | `not_applicable` — a stage move is state, not an ask of a person; the board is where the work is seen | `not_applicable` — scoring and routing are deterministic computations, and routing a lead to a target is not a task somebody accepted | `not_applicable` — a quote approval is already a governed human decision with its own record; a task beside it would be a second queue for one ask | `not_applicable` — a signature request is an *external* party's action, not internal work, and a provider event is not a person | `partial` — activation raises pending delivery and service obligations, which are the domain's own explicit handoff records. A follow-up task on an unhandled obligation is a plausible future consumer and is deliberately **not** built here | `deferred` — M14b2's `delivery-commercial-change` already hands off through Lifecycle, which now opens the task. Consuming the capability a second time from Delivery would create two tasks for one human ask; revisit with M16b |\n364\t| **Opaque subject envelope** — a record referenced across a seam carries resource, id, owner and provenance rather than a typed foreign key | `not_applicable` — no cross-domain reference | `aligned` by construction — Intelligence acts on the host `lead` and claims no ownership of it | `not_applicable` | `not_applicable` | `partial` — `contract-activation` stores typed ids to records it owns, which is correct; the envelope applies only where the target table varies | `partial` — same reason: `deliveryProjectId` and friends are typed and correct within one package |\n365\t\n366\t### The three domains outside the six-column table\n367\t\n368\t| Domain | Status against Work v1 |\n369\t|---|---|\n370\t| **Core CRM (Sales) / Lead** | `aligned` — `lead.qualify` opens exactly one follow-up with a host-owned subject, through the exported creator rather than the capability, because `PackageRegistry.capability({ consumer })` resolves against *registered packages* and a host action is not one. Published as `HOST_ACTIONS_CANNOT_DECLARE_CAPABILITIES`; it is the same code, not a second path |\n371\t| **Service** | `aligned` — `record-escalation` opens one task through `work/follow-up@1` when composed with `followUp: true`. **`support-case-activity` stays domain-specific and is not migrated.** It is a support-case-scoped log with its own visibility model, its own types (`customer_reply`, `internal_note`, `transition`) and its own sequence; folding it into Work's four-entry vocabulary would either lose those distinctions or force Work's vocabulary open, and an open vocabulary is not a curated timeline. Two timelines that mean different things are honest; one that means both is not |\n372\t| **Lifecycle** | `aligned` — `request-commercial-followup` opens one task through the capability when composed with `followUp: true`. The subject is package-owned and the envelope says so |\n373\t| **Custom-package fixture** (`partner-scorecard`) | `not_applicable` — it rates a partner from a policy. Nothing about a rating is an ask of a person, and adding one to demonstrate the seam would be a fixture pretending to be a business |\n374\t| **Marketing & Growth** | `not_applicable` — **task and journey work remains unimplemented.** `MARKETING_GROWTH_OPERATIONS.md` and `CAMPAIGNS_JOURNEYS.md` plan campaign tasks and durable journey steps; none of it exists, MK4 is hard-blocked on `JOBS_AND_OUTBOX.md`, and a journey step is a *scheduled* thing, which Work v1 explicitly is not. No Marketing row can be assessed and none is claimed |\n375\t\n376\t### What Work v1 deliberately did not close\n377\t\n378\t- **Delivery history is not unified.** Delivery's change requests, plan\n379\t  revisions, deliverables and acceptance evidence remain its own records with\n380\t  their own semantics. Work v1 adds no cross-domain reader and no aggregation:\n381\t  `JTBD-DO-08` is *partially supported* for a **subject** timeline, and the row\n382\t  says in the same breath that it is not unified.\n383\t- **No existing domain-specific activity record is migrated.** Service's\n384\t  `support-case-activity` is the named case; the rule is general.\n385\t- **Nothing is scheduled.** The `deferred` cell above is about a *consumer*, not\n386\t  about a timer. There is no scheduler in this repository and Work v1 does not\n387\t  bring one.\n388\t\n389\t## The M16a hardening backfill answer, as the rule requires\n390\t\n391\tThe M16a post-merge hardening introduced one horizontal change: a **shared\n392\tcalendar-date authority** in `packages/core/src/validation.js`\n393\t(`isCalendarDate`, `requireCalendarDate`, `calendarDaysBetween`). A calendar\n394\tdate is a different kind of fact from an instant, `Date.parse` alone accepts\n395\tdays that never existed, and the round-trip rule that refuses them had been\n396\tindependently re-implemented in **four** packages. A validator carries no domain\n397\tvocabulary and is a runtime primitive, which is the ADR-018 test for what core\n398\tmay own.\n399\t\n400\t| Question | Answer |\n401\t|---|---|\n402\t| Which old domains does this touch? | Every domain that stores a `YYYY-MM-DD` value: **Contract Activation**, **Delivery**, **Service** and **Lifecycle**. Pipeline, Lead Intelligence, Commercial Ops and Signature & Order store no calendar date and are `not_applicable` |\n403\t| Which are already aligned? | **Contract Activation** and **Lifecycle** — both now delegate to the core authority; `packages/contracts/src/dates.js` re-exports it so M12's public surface is unchanged |\n404\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n405\t| Which need a code backfill? | **Delivery** (`packages/delivery/src/dates.js`) and **Service** (`calendarDate` in `packages/service/src/service-actions.js`) keep their own copies of the identical round-trip rule. They are `partial`: the rule they apply is the same rule and no behaviour differs today, but it is a second and third implementation of it. Declared here, not closed here — extraction and consolidation of a legacy implementation is sequenced work, not something a defect-fix PR does on the way past |\n406\t| Was the matrix updated? | Yes — the row below, and this section |\n407\t\n408\t| Horizontal capability | Pipeline | Lead Intelligence | Commercial Ops | Signature & Order | Contract Activation | Delivery |\n409\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n410\t| **Shared calendar-date authority** — one round-trip validator for `YYYY-MM-DD`, refusing days that never existed | `not_applicable` — stores no calendar date | `not_applicable` — same | `not_applicable` — same | `not_applicable` — same | `aligned` — delegates to `packages/core` and re-exports it | `partial` — keeps an identical private copy in `src/dates.js` |\n411\t\n412\tService is `partial` for the same reason as Delivery; Lifecycle is `aligned`.\n413\t\n414\t## The M15 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n415\t\n416\tM15 introduced one horizontal change: a **second capability on an existing\n417\tpackage**, `contracts/service-obligations@1`, and with it the first evolution of\n418\ta shipped M12 record (`service-obligation` → revision 2, gaining `coverageRef`\n419\tand `activatedAt` and an `activated` status).\n420\t\n421\t| Question | Answer |\n422\t|---|---|\n423\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **Contracts only.** It gains a capability and a record revision; nothing else in the repository changed shape |\n424\t| Which are already aligned? | Contracts is `aligned` on every seam row and stays so: the capability is additive, `delivery-obligations@1` is byte-identical, and `packageContract: 1` did not move |\n425\t| Which need metadata only? | **None.** No other domain gained a concept it must now declare |\n426\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None.** Service is a new package and owns its own surface; the three `needs_extraction` domains are untouched and no closer to extraction than before |\n427\t| Was the matrix updated? | Yes — the Service row above, and this section |\n428\t\n429\tThe honest note: M15 is *not* evidence that the seam is finished. It is the\n430\tthird package and the second consumer, and what it showed the seam still cannot\n431\texpress is the input to step 3 of the sequencing below.\n432\t\n433\t## The DX4 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n434\t\n435\tDX4 (`crm package test`) is a **horizontal capability**: it applies to every\n436\tpackage, including the three domains that are not packages yet.\n437\t\n438\t| Question | Answer |\n439\t|---|---|\n440\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **None at runtime.** DX4 adds a CLI command and moves three pieces of shared CLI logic into their own modules. No kernel behaviour changes, no domain is refactored |\n441\t| Which are already aligned? | The three first-party packages — Contracts, Delivery, Service — pass `crm package test` mechanically, which is stronger than the prose \"aligned\" that preceded it. The custom-package fixture **does not**: it acts on a record `delivery` owns without declaring `delivery`, and no capability of `delivery` expresses that. A real seam gap, reported rather than rescued |\n442\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n443\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None today.** The three `needs_extraction` domains cannot be run through DX4 at all: they are not packages, so there is nothing for it to compose. That is not a new gap — it is the same gap, now measurable |\n444\t| What changed for extraction? | DX4 is the gate the extraction pilot was waiting for. An extracted domain is a package, and a package that cannot pass `crm package test` has not been extracted, only moved |\n445\t| Matrix updated? | Yes — this section, the conformance row below and the revised extraction gate |\n446\t\n447\tOne row of the matrix changes meaning rather than status: **\"JTBD rows with\n448\tlinked evidence\"** and the package-seam rows were previously argued from prose\n449\tand per-package suites. For the four packages they are now argued from a\n450\tmechanical run whose output is a stable document. The three legacy domains stay\n451\texactly where they were.\n452\t\n453\t### `service` had no conformance coverage until now\n454\t\n455\t`assertPackageConforms` was called three times — for `contracts`, `delivery` and\n456\t`partner-scorecard`. The newest package, the one M15 built and the one this\n457\tmatrix cites as validating the seam, had **none**. Anything this document said\n458\tabout \"every package conforms\" was untrue of `service` at the moment it was\n459\twritten. DX4's official matrix covers all four, and its suite fails if a package\n460\tis added without being listed.\n461\t\n462\t## The DX3 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n463\t\n464\tDX3 (`crm package scaffold`) is a **horizontal capability** in the same sense\n465\tDX4 is: it applies to every package that could exist, including the three\n466\tdomains that are not packages yet.\n467\t\n468\t| Question | Answer |\n469\t|---|---|\n470\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **None, at runtime or on disk.** DX3 adds one CLI command and one test file. It changes no kernel behaviour, refactors no domain, and its own output is written only where a caller points it |\n471\t| Which are already aligned? | Not the right question for this capability. DX3 produces *new* packages; the four existing ones were written before it and none is regenerated. What is aligned is the **shape**: scaffolded output passes the same `crm package test` matrix the four packages are held to, so DX4's contract is now the default starting point rather than something an author has to remember |\n472\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n473\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None.** The three `needs_extraction` domains cannot be scaffolded any more than they can be conformance-tested — they are not packages. DX3 does not change that, and a scaffold is explicitly *not* an extraction tool: it writes an empty package, never a migration of an existing one |\n474\t| What changed for extraction? | The pilot gains a starting point but no permission. An extraction now begins `crm package scaffold lead-intelligence --apply` and proceeds by moving code into it, with `crm package test` as the gate. Every precondition recorded below is unchanged and still unproved |\n475\t| Matrix updated? | Yes — this section and the re-evaluated ordering below |\n476\t\n477\t### What DX3 deliberately did not close\n478\t\n479\t- **No domain semantics.** The scaffold generates no record, action, policy,\n480\t  capability, provider, Admin section, Solution Plan or MCP tool. That is a\n481\t  decision, not an omission: a generated field is a claim about a business\n482\t  nobody described, and an agent reads generated code as a decision already\n483\t  taken.\n484\t- **No composition.** `packages/domains/generated/index.js` is still edited by\n485\t  hand. Automating it would remove the one deliberate human act in the\n486\t  package-installation path.\n487\t- **No migration, no database, no install, no publish, no registry.**\n488\t- **No HTTP-route contribution.** Still the open seam DX4 identified, still a\n489\t  precondition for Commercial and Signature specifically, and still untouched.\n490\t\n491\t## The DX1 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n492\t\n493\tDX1 (`accordo project doctor`) is a **horizontal capability**: it applies to every\n494\tproject built on this framework, and to every domain in one.\n495\t\n496\t| Question | Answer |\n497\t|---|---|\n498\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **None at runtime.** DX1 adds one CLI command, two CLI modules and a test file. It changes no kernel behaviour, refactors no domain, mutates nothing and executes no project source in its own process |\n499\t| Which are already aligned? | All of them, in the only sense the command measures: it reports composition, package-boundary, module-state, plan, Skill, docs-link and hygiene health for whatever the project contains, and the three `needs_extraction` domains are ordinary kernel source to it. Its first run on this repository is **0 failures** with three real warnings — two stale Solution Plans and six one-sided Skill mirrors |\n500\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n501\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None.** The doctor asks existing authorities; it introduces no rule any domain must now satisfy |\n502\t| What changed for extraction? | It becomes a precondition of the *process* rather than of the seam: an extraction should start from a project whose coherence is machine-checked, and should be re-checked after. It does not remove any of the four open blockers |\n503\t| Matrix updated? | Yes — this section, and the extraction gate now maintained in `EXTRACTION_PREPARATION.md` |\n504\t\n505\t### What DX1 measured that this matrix had only asserted\n506\t\n507\tTwo rows of this document were prose until now and are now mechanical:\n508\t\n509\t- **the Skill mirrors.** This matrix records `partial` because six domain build\n510\t  skills exist under `.claude/` only. `accordo project doctor` reports that as\n511\t  `skills.mirror-coverage: warning` with the six named, and would report a\n512\t  **failure** if two mirrors of one skill ever disagreed in content. DX2 still\n513\t  owns the fix; the gap is now observed on every run rather than remembered.\n514\t- **Solution Plan currency.** Two of the three checked-in plans no longer bind to\n515\t  the current composition. That was true before this PR — verified identical on\n516\t  `main@5da5205` — and nothing reported it. It is now `plans.*: warning`.\n517\t\n518\t### What DX1 deliberately did not close\n519\t\n520\t- **No mutation, no `--fix`.** Every finding names the existing command that\n521\t  would fix it.\n522\t- **No domain behaviour, no database, no provider health, no production\n523\t  readiness.** All named limitations in the report itself.\n524\t- **No package conformance run.** `PACKAGE_CONFORMANCE_NOT_RUN` points at\n525\t  `crm package test`.\n526\t- **No generated-source drift beyond what a generator contract proves.** A\n527\t  fuzzy comparison that cries wolf is a check people silence.\n528\t\n529\t## The Project Bootstrap answer: **not horizontal**, and why that is the finding\n530\t\n531\t`create-accordo` (`packages/create-accordo`, `projectBootstrapContract: 1`) is\n532\trecorded here **because the rule's failure mode is silence.** An unrecorded \"not\n533\thorizontal\" is indistinguishable from a forgotten one, so the judgement is\n534\twritten down where somebody can disagree with it in one place rather than\n535\treconstruct it from the diff.\n536\t\n537\t**The judgement: it is not a horizontal capability, and no domain row exists for\n538\tit.** The rule's test is *\"one every domain could use\"*. This sits one level\n539\t**above** domains: it creates the container a domain lives in. It composes none\n540\tof them — the generated `packages/domains/generated/index.js` is the empty one\n541\tthis repository ships — and it introduces no contract, check or discipline that\n542\ta domain is measured against. The question a matrix row would have to answer,\n543\t*\"is Commercial Operations aligned with project bootstrap?\"*, has no meaning in\n544\tthe way *\"is it aligned with the package seam?\"* does.\n545\t\n546\tContrast the three that **did** declare a horizontal answer, each for a reason\n547\tthis one lacks: DX4 introduced a conformance contract every package is held to;\n548\tDX3 made the shape every new package starts from; DX1 introduced findings graded\n549\tagainst every project. This introduces a way to *obtain* a project, and then gets\n550\tout of the way.\n551\t\n552\t| Question | Answer |\n553\t|---|---|\n554\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **None, at runtime or on disk.** It adds one package, one test file and documentation. It changes no kernel behaviour, refactors no domain, executes no project source, opens no database and mutates nothing outside a caller-chosen empty directory |\n555\t| Which are already aligned? | Not a question this capability asks. Every domain's source is copied into a generated project as inert files; none of them is composed, so none is graded |\n556\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n557\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None** |\n558\t| What changed for extraction? | **Nothing.** Every precondition in `EXTRACTION_PREPARATION.md` is unchanged and still unproved. A bootstrap produces empty projects; it moves no domain out of `packages/core` and it is not an extraction tool |\n559\t| Matrix updated? | Yes — this section, recording the decision rather than a row |\n560\t\n561\t### What it deliberately did not close\n562\t\n563\t- **No domain composition.** A project that arrived carrying somebody else's\n564\t  Lead model is the DX3 \"rich template\" mistake at project scale, and it is\n565\t  refused for the same reason: a generated domain is a claim about a business\n566\t  nobody described.\n567\t- **No upgrade path.** The framework is vendored, so a generated project\n568\t  upgrades by merging rather than by bumping a version. Closing that needs a\n569\t  published, versioned framework package — a human decision, not a code gap.\n570\t- **No publication.** The npm names remain empty reservations. The source\n571\t  scaffolds; the registry does not.\n572\t\n573\t## The DX6 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n574\t\n575\tDX6 (`accordo scenario run`) is a **horizontal capability**: business-scenario\n576\tevidence is something every domain could have, and this matrix already carries a\n577\t\"JTBD rows with linked evidence\" row that DX6 turns from prose into a report.\n578\t\n579\t| Question | Answer |\n580\t|---|---|\n581\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **None at runtime.** DX6 adds one CLI command, three CLI modules, one checked-in scenario document and two test files. A requirement no plan states cannot be caught here.\n657\t- **It drives no browser**, contacts no provider, opens no database, and observes\n658\t  no deployed or external system — the same gaps DX5 and DX6 publish, published\n659\t  again here rather than assumed to carry over.\n660\t- **It does not make PROVE complete.** Both shipped consumers exit 1. That is the\n661\t  true state of both plans, and a green exit here would be the defect.\n662\t\n663\t## The LA0 backfill answer, as the rule requires\n664\t\n665\tLA0 (the Lead Intelligence characterization harness) is **not** a horizontal\n666\tcapability in the sense the rule usually means — it adds no kernel behaviour and\n667\tno contract any domain must satisfy. It is recorded here because it changes what\n668\tthis matrix can claim about one domain.\n669\t\n670\t| Question | Answer |\n671\t|---|---|\n672\t| Which old domains does this touch? | **Lead Intelligence only**, and only by observing it. No code moved, no helper was relocated, no ambient field replaced, no seam added |\n673\t| Which are already aligned? | Not the frame. What changed is coverage: Lead Intelligence now has a frozen, machine-checked record of its externally observable behaviour — 149 observations, 779 asserted values. Commercial Operations and Signature & Order have **none** |\n674\t| Which need metadata only? | **None** |\n675\t| Which need a code backfill? | **None from LA0 itself.** It surfaced two `defect_candidate` findings in `record-signal` — an unbounded `value` and control characters stored verbatim — which were fixed in their own PR, before the extraction rather than during it |\n676\t| What changed for extraction? | The acceptance criterion — behaviour preservation proved from the outside — is mechanical for the first time. It was the last unknown; what remains is decisions |\n677\t| Matrix updated? | Yes — this section |\n678\t\n679\t### Characterization coverage, by domain\n680\t\n681\t| Domain | Characterized | Note |\n682\t|---|---|---|\n683\t| Lead Intelligence | **yes** — `tests/characterization/`, `legacyCharacterizationContract: 1` | the extraction candidate. Still **not** package-aligned: it remains kernel source with an ambient runtime field and a fixed definition slot |\n684\t| Commercial Operations | no | would need its own suite before any extraction |\n685\t| Signature & Order | no | same, plus the HTTP-route seam it owns |\n686\t| Contracts, Delivery, Service | **not applicable** | package-native from birth; `crm package test` plus their own suites already cover them |\n687\t\n688\t**LA0 is a gate for extracting a legacy domain, not a requirement for building a\n689\tnew one.** A package written package-native has no pre-move behaviour to\n690\tpreserve, and demanding a characterization baseline from it would be ceremony.\n691\t\n692\t## Sequencing, which this document does not change\n693\t\n694\t```text\n695\t1.  finish, independently review and merge M14b2      done (PR #25)\n696\t2.  M15 — Service package, built on the seam          ← where we are\n697\t3.  review the M15 learnings: what the seam still cannot express\n698\t4.  a controlled Legacy Domain Alignment Pass, one domain at a time\n699\t```\n700\t\n701\tSteps 2 and 3 come before step 4 on purpose. Contracts and Delivery are two\n702\tdata points, and Delivery is the only one that has *consumed* another package's\n703\tcapability. Service is the third — and the first built with the seam already\n704\tmature. Extracting a legacy domain before that learning bakes today's shape into\n705\tfour more places.\n706\t\n707\t**DX4 (`crm package test`) is the practical gate.** An extraction without a\n708\tconformance harness is a refactor whose only proof is that the existing tests\n709\tstill pass — which is precisely the proof that misses a boundary violation. The\n710\troadmap already sequences DX3 and DX4 after M15, and the extraction pilot after\n711\tDX4.\n712\t\n713\t### Lead Intelligence as the first extraction — a hypothesis, pending evidence\n714\t\n715\t**This is a working hypothesis, not a decision.** It is recorded so the eventual\n716\tchoice is argued against a written position rather than made ad hoc.\n717\t\n718\tWhy it is the plausible first candidate:\n719\t\n720\t- **Its dependency direction is inward.** Intelligence reads CRM records and\n721\t  writes its own evidence. It is not read by Commercial, Signature or Delivery,\n722\t  so extracting it should not require a new capability for anyone else.\n723\t- **It already has the shape.** Versioned fingerprinted definitions (ADR-015),\n724\t  managed append-only evidence, a reproducible historical decision — the\n725\t  properties a package must prove are already proven for it.\n726\t- **Its blast radius is bounded.** Scoring and routing are self-contained;\n727\t  Commercial and Signature carry money and an external provider.\n728\t- **Its failure mode is legible.** A scoring or routing regression is visible in\n729\t  a reproduction test, not months later in a ledger.\n730\t\n731\tWhat must be true before it is chosen — none of which is established today:\n732\t\n733\t1. DX4 exists and a package can prove conformance mechanically.\n734\t2. M15's learnings are reviewed and the seam has whatever they showed it needs.\n735\t3. A real dependency audit shows nothing reads Intelligence internals — asserted\n736\t   above from the layout, **not yet proved by a tool**.\n737\t4. Every historical routing decision still reproduces identically across the\n738\t   move. Behavior preservation is the entire acceptance criterion.\n739\t\n740\tIf any of those fails, another domain is the pilot, or none is. **Nothing in this\n741\tdocument authorizes starting an extraction.**\n742\t\n743\t### The three candidates, measured (M15 review)\n744\t\n745\tThe hypothesis above was written before M15 shipped. The M15 review measured the\n746\tthree candidates rather than reasoning about them, so the eventual decision is\n747\targued against numbers. **Still planning only: nothing here starts an\n748\textraction, and no extraction work is in PR #27.**\n749\t\n750\t| | Lead Intelligence | Commercial Operations | Signature & Order |\n751\t|---|---|---|---|\n752\t| kernel source to move | 1,313 lines, 2 files | 1,393 lines, 3 files | 1,488 lines, 2 files |\n753\t| files outside the domain that name it | 11 | 16 | 10 |\n754\t| dedicated HTTP routes in the kernel server | none | `POST /api/catalog/sync` | `POST /api/signature/providers/:provider/events`, `POST /api/signature/envelopes/:id/reconcile` |\n755\t| dedicated method on the app object | none | `syncCatalog` | `ingestSignatureEvent`, `reconcileSignature` |\n756\t| `/api/schema` block | `intelligence` | `commercial` | `signature` |\n757\t| Admin files that read its schema block | 0 | 6 | 3 |\n758\t| carries money | no | **yes** (`commercial-money.js`, 416 lines) | yes, by reference |\n759\t| calls an external provider | yes, in a `prepare` phase the kernel already isolates | yes, outside the write transaction | **yes, and it receives inbound webhooks** |\n760\t| depended on by another domain | no | **yes** — Contracts activates an Order; Delivery and Service reach obligations raised from it | yes — Contracts reads the signed Order |\n761\t| what a regression looks like | a reproduction test disagrees | a stored amount disagrees | an envelope or artifact is lost, or a webhook is accepted twice |\n762\t\n763\tThree things the table says that prose did not:\n764\t\n765\t1. **Intelligence is the only candidate with no kernel HTTP surface, no app\n766\t   method and no Admin coupling.** Its extraction is a package boundary and\n767\t   nothing else. Commercial and Signature each require moving a route out of\n768\t   `apps/server`, which is a second, unrelated piece of work — and one the\n769\t   package seam does not currently support at all: **no package can contribute\n770\t   an HTTP route today.** That is a seam gap, not a scheduling problem.\n771\t2. **Commercial is the most depended-upon.** Contracts, Delivery and Service all\n772\t   sit downstream of the Order it produces. Moving it first means designing\n773\t   three capability edges at once, on the domain that carries money.\n774\t3. **Signature is the riskiest for a reason unrelated to size.** It is the only\n775\t   domain that accepts bytes from outside. An extraction that changes where the\n776\t   verification code lives is an extraction that has to re-prove replay,\n777\t   out-of-order and wrong-tenant handling — the evidence hardest to be confident\n778\t   about after a move.\n779\t\n780\t**Recommendation: Lead Intelligence, at moderate confidence.** Moderate rather\n781\tthan high because two of its four preconditions are still unproved — DX4 does\n782\tnot exist, and \"nothing reads Intelligence internals\" is read off the file\n783\tlayout rather than established by a tool. The measurement strengthens the\n784\thypothesis; it does not convert it into a decision.\n785\t\n786\t**A precondition the table added:** before Commercial or Signature could ever be\n787\ta candidate, the package seam needs a way for a package to own an HTTP route.\n788\tNeither is extractable today at any confidence, and that is a finding about the\n789\t*seam*, not about them.\n790\t\n791\t### Recommended next ordering\n792\t\n793\tOrdering, with the reason each item sits where it does. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01M6ZV5dCjegm1SkRBsRKoys","type":"tool_result","content":"## ADR-018 — The core runtime is a platform; domain behavior belongs in domain packages\n\n**Status:** accepted (Platform Alignment Gate, after Milestone 11).\n\n**Context.** Eleven milestones put an increasing amount of *domain* behavior inside `packages/core/src`: `intelligence-registry.js`, `intelligence-actions.js`, `commercial-registry.js`, `commercial-money.js`, `catalog-sync.js`, `commercial-actions.js`, `signature-registry.js` and `signature-operations.js` sit next to genuinely generic machinery like `database.js`, `audit.js`, `event-bus.js`, `module-manifest.js`, `action-runtime.js` and `external-operation.js`. Each addition was individually reasonable — the runtime pieces they needed did not exist yet — but the trend has one ending: a core that every project must adopt whole, where lead scoring, pricing and signature semantics are framework concerns. The next three domains (Contract/Subscription, Delivery, Service) would triple it.\n\n**Decision.** The core is a **platform runtime**, not a CRM. Domain behavior belongs in optional domain packages built on the runtime's contracts.\n\n**The core runtime may own** module and runtime contracts; the deterministic database boundary; transactions and savepoints; audit; trace; the event-buffer/outbox contract; the action and workflow runtimes; the external-operation runtime; provider and definition registries (the *mechanism*, not any particular provider kind); policy-version and fingerprint helpers; the module factory; the schema contract; plugin and capability contracts; and the generic HTTP, SDK and Admin adapters.\n\n**Domain packages own** Lead Intelligence, Commercial Operations, Signature/Documents, Contract/Subscription, Delivery, Service and Analytics — their records, their policies, their provider kinds and their actions.\n\nA provisional package shape, deliberately **not** a naming decision (the public name is still an open human decision, `MASTER_PLAN.md` §10):\n\n```text\n@<brand>/runtime        database, transactions, audit, trace, events, registries\n@<brand>/framework      module factory, manifest, generated API/SDK/Admin adapters\n@<brand>/intelligence   enrichment, scoring, routing\n@<brand>/commercial     catalog, pricing, quotes, discount policy\n@<brand>/signature      envelopes, verified events, signed artifacts, orders\n@<brand>/subscription   contracts, subscriptions, terms, entitlements, renewal\n@<brand>/delivery       projects, milestones, work packages, acceptance\n@<brand>/service        service contracts, SLA, support cases\n@<brand>/analytics      semantic metrics, reports, dashboards\n```\n\n**The core budget rule.** *New domain-specific business behavior is not added to `packages/core` unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability.* \"Proven\" means at least two domains need it, or it is one of the responsibilities listed above. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say in its description which runtime capability it is and why the domain package cannot own it. This rule is a review gate, not a lint.\n\n**Staged extraction, never a big-bang refactor.** Existing M9–M11 files stay exactly where they are; this ADR moves nothing:\n\n1. **New domains start outside core.** Milestone 12 onward builds domain packages against runtime contracts from day one.\n2. **Shared capability contracts are introduced where a second consumer proves the need** (`docs/strategy/PLATFORM_CAPABILITIES.md`), not speculatively.\n3. **Existing domain code moves only through behavior-preserving PRs** — one domain at a time, import paths and public behavior unchanged, the full suite green before and after.\n4. **Compatibility is maintained** with golden tests: the same starter, the same manifests, the same HTTP/SDK responses, the same audit/event/trace shapes.\n\nAn extraction PR that changes behavior is not an extraction PR.\n\n**Plugin ownership.** A third-party package must be able to add domain modules, provider kinds, actions and policies **without patching core**: it registers through the same checked-in `generated/index.js` registries the first-party domains use, and it is bound by the same capability contract. If a plugin needs a core change to work, that is a missing runtime capability and belongs in this ADR's list — not in the plugin.\n\n**Consequences.** Domain packages become optional: a project that only wants Lead Intelligence should not carry pricing or signature code. The core gains a stable, testable surface. Extraction costs several careful PRs, and until they land the tree keeps the current shape — which is exactly why this ADR is written before the next domain rather than after it.\n\n### ADR-018 addendum — the domain seam, proven by Milestone 12\n\n**Status:** accepted (Milestone 12, the first domain built outside core).\n\nMilestone 12 was the test the ADR asked for: build a whole domain — contracts,\nsubscriptions and obligations — in `packages/contracts/` without adding a\ndomain concept to core. It needed exactly **two** generic runtime additions,\nboth of which pass the core budget rule because they name no domain:\n\n**1. A domain registry seam (`packages/core/src/domain-registry.js`).**\n*Superseded by addendum 3: the file is now\n`packages/core/src/package-registry.js` and the field is `packageContract`.\nThe paragraph below records what Milestone 12 shipped.* A\ndomain package hands the application a plain declaration —\n`{name, domainContract: 1, label, actions[], policies[{kind, definition}],\nmetadata()}` — and the runtime does what it already does for every other\nregistry: validates the shape, refuses duplicates, computes the ADR-015\ndeclared-definition fingerprint of each policy, persists it in\n`definition_versions` under `domain-policy:<domain>:<kind>`, registers the\nactions, resolves policies by explicit `(domain, kind, name, version)` from a\nMap, and publishes function-free metadata under `/api/schema` → `domains`.\nThe word \"contract\", \"subscription\" and \"obligation\" appears nowhere in\n`packages/core`; a test scans the core sources to keep it that way. Removing\nthe single static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js` removes the\ndomain, and the kernel boots byte-identically without it.\n\n**2. A strict `boolean` action input type.** The action runtime had `string`,\n`timestamp`, `enum`, `integer` and `json`; a boolean had to travel as JSON,\nwhich is a modelling accident rather than a decision. The new type accepts\n`true`/`false` only — never `\"true\"`, `1` or `\"yes\"` — so a term flag cannot be\nset by a string that happens to look truthy.\n\n**Nothing else was needed**, and that is the finding: the module factory,\nmanaged writes, the record-action runtime, transactions, audit, events and\ntrace carried a full domain unchanged. The seam is generic by construction —\nthe next domain package (Delivery, Service) registers through the same\n`DomainRegistries` without a further core change, and a third-party package\nuses the identical path, as ADR-018's plugin-ownership clause requires.\n\n**What the seam deliberately does not do.** It does not sequence domains, share\nstate between them, let one domain override another's action, or give a domain\nprivileged database access: a domain package writes exclusively through the\nsame managed record services every other action uses.\n\n### ADR-018 addendum 2 — what the Milestone 12 adversarial review corrected\n\n**Status:** accepted (adversarial review of PR #16, before merge).\n\nThree defects in the first M12 implementation were domain-model defects rather\nthan bugs, and all three are the same mistake: **claiming more than the\nevidence supports.**\n\n**1. A term recorded after signature is not a signed term.** The signed\ndocument package (ADR-017) contains priced lines, parties and signers — and no\nterm, renewal clause or notice period. The first implementation collected\n`effectiveDate`, `termStartDate`, `termEndDate`, `autoRenew` and\n`renewalNoticeDays` at activation and stored them as \"the contract term\",\nwhich reads as though the customer had signed them.\n\nThe honest bounded correction, chosen over adding pre-signature terms (that is\na Quote/Document feature, not a review fix): the values are **operational\nactivation metadata**. Every record that stores them also stores\n`termsSource: \"post-signature-operational-activation\"` and a required human\n`termsReason`; the plan payload, `/api/schema`, the Admin form and the guide\nall say the term is not part of the signed agreement; and JTBD CS-01 is\nnarrowed to operational activation rather than claiming a signed commercial\nagreement end to end.\n\n*The follow-up this defers, deliberately:* a pre-signature commercial-term\nsnapshot (Quote Version → document package → Order → Contract, with equality\nvalidated at activation) is the correct long-term answer. It changes what is\nsigned, so it belongs to a Quote/Signature milestone with its own review.\n\n**2. Classification has two dimensions, not one.** The first implementation\ngave each Order Component exactly one label from\n`subscription | delivery | service | other`. Annual premium support is a\nrecurring commercial commitment **and** a future service obligation; one label\nhad to drop one of them, and it dropped the subscription line — silently\nremoving real recurring money from the Subscription.\n\nClassification is now two independent axes:\n\n```js\n{ commercialActivation: 'subscription' | 'non_subscription' | 'ambiguous',\n  obligations: ['delivery'] | ['service'] | ['delivery','service'] | [] | 'ambiguous',\n  reason }\n```\n\nEvery component still becomes exactly one Contract Line. A component may\ncreate a Subscription Line *and* an obligation, one, or neither. Either axis\nmay be `ambiguous`, which blocks activation until a human resolves **that\naxis** with its own reason; overrides are therefore dimension-specific, and\nboth the policy's answer and the human's are stored per axis. The vocabulary\nstays bounded — two enums and a two-element set, not a rule DSL.\n\n**3. Nothing is `active` before it starts.** A future-dated term produced a\ncontract and subscription with status `active`. They are now `scheduled` until\nthe business date reaches `termStartDate`, and the schema, the Admin and the\nguide state plainly that **nothing transitions them** — there is no scheduler.\nA term that already ended is refused (`TERM_ALREADY_ENDED`) rather than\nrecorded as active history.\n\nDeciding this needed a business date, so the runtime gained one more generic\ncapability: **an injectable application clock** (`createAgentCrmApp({ clock })`,\nthreaded through the action and external-operation runtimes). It names no\ndomain, it makes every time-dependent action reproducible in tests, and it is\nthe third and last addition M12 asked of the kernel.\n\n**Also corrected:** a renewal notice period without `autoRenew` is refused\nrather than stored as a clause that can never apply.\n\n### ADR-018 addendum 3 — the public domain-package contract\n\n**Status:** accepted (Milestone 13, the second package and the first customer-authored one).\n\nADR-018 said domain behavior belongs in optional packages. Milestone 12 proved\none package could exist outside the kernel. This addendum makes the seam a\n**public contract**: the way a customer's own package attaches is the way the\nfirst-party packages attach, and there is no second, privileged mechanism.\n\n**The contract.** A package exports one static definition:\n\n```js\ndefinePackage({\n  packageContract: 1,        // the contract it is written against\n  name: 'delivery',          // canonical, unique, Map-keyed","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"bef03715-d88c-4975-af87-f0660561ba3a","timestamp":"2026-08-13T23:53:57.718Z","subagent_type":"Explore","task_description":"Research partner-management build approach"}
