{"type":"system","subtype":"task_notification","task_id":"a081d76c0f9abebf8","tool_use_id":"toolu_01JutJr96kqVz3u44j56EF6n","status":"completed","output_file":"/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-agent-crm-panel-runs-TS-12-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1-fixture/dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6/tasks/a081d76c0f9abebf8.output","summary":"# Research Summary: Building a `packages/partners` Domain Package\n\n## 1. `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` — the package contract\n\nFull path: `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-12-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`\n\n**When a package is justified** (§1, lines 30-40): only when it owns *all* of: >1 related resource, cross-record invariants, actions/workflows enforcing them, evidence records, and a real install/remove boundary. A Partner record with an onboarding→active→suspended→offboarded lifecycle plus related resources (e.g. contacts, agreements) clears this bar; a single object would just be a module.\n\n**Scaffold first** (§1): `npm run crm -- package scaffold partners --apply` writes exactly `src/index.js` + `README.md` with empty declarations — start here rather than copying another domain.\n\n**Identity** (§2): `packageContract: 1`, `name` matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$` (unique, Map key, appears in `/api/schema`), `version` (positive integer, own to the package), `label`.\n\n**Declaring dependencies** (§3): only via `requires: [{ package, capability, version }]`, resolved at startup (missing/version-mismatch/cycle stops the app naming the edge). Opened inside an action via `domains.capability({ consumer, capability, version, context: { modules, actor, now } })` — this runs inside the *caller's* transaction. Offering one: `capabilities: [{ name, version, description, create({modules,actor,now}) {...} }]`.\n\n**Resources** (§4): ordinary manifests applied via `npm run crm -- module create packages/<name>/modules/<record>.module.json --apply`, declared in `resources: [...]` on the package (collision across packages = startup failure). Evidence records: every field `\"writable\": \"managed\"` → module exposes only `get`/`list` publicly, mutated only through package actions.\n\n**Critical distinction — host-record dependency vs package dependency** (§4, lines 164-192): if your package acts on a record the *project* owns (e.g. a `lead`, `company`) — **do not list it in `resources`**; that's a host-record dependency reached via the action runtime, not `domains.capability`. Only list in `resources` what your package itself owns. This matters for Partner: the Partner record itself belongs to your new package, but if a Partner action needs to touch `delivery-partner-engagement` (owned by `packages/delivery`), that's a capability of the `delivery` package you'd need to declare (or, per the rule below, delivery's `delivery-partner-engagement` record is owned by `delivery`, not a host record — so touching it requires a declared capability from `delivery`, and `delivery` currently offers no such capability — see §5 below).\n\n**Actions/policies** (§5): actions are ordinary action definitions (`docs/ACTIONS.md`) — same runtime/transaction/audit/trace. Versioned decisions → policies with JSON-safe `config`, deterministic/synchronous/total handlers, deep-frozen input, fingerprinted (ADR-015).\n\n**Metadata** (§6): `metadata()` returns plain function-free JSON-safe data only, called once at startup, published at `/api/schema`. Must include a `notModeled` list. May **never** restate registry-computed keys (`version`, `resources`, `requires`, `provides`, `actions`, `policies`, `label`, `description`) — refused at startup.\n\n**Registration** (§8): one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js` — the *only* place a project names packages. No dynamic loading, `eval`, or marketplace.\n\n**Public kernel surface** (§10): import only from `packages/core/index.js`; everything under `packages/core/src/*` is private and `package validate` fails on any reach-in (including via `import()`/`require()`).\n\n**Conformance** (§11-13): \n```\ncrm package scaffold   → empty conforming package\ncrm module create      → each owned record\ncrm package validate   → structural check (imports & runs your src/index.js)\ncrm package inspect    → what it declares/owns/offers/needs\ncrm package test       → composes into a throwaway project, boots twice (with/without)\n```\n`crm package test` fails with `UNDECLARED_PACKAGE_RECORD_DEPENDENCY` if an action targets another *package's* record without a declared capability of that package.\n\n**Tests/evidence** (§12): reuse `tests/helpers/package-conformance.js`; add an e2e test booting a real project and driving the action over HTTP/SDK; prove optionality (absence test); update `docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` conservatively; ship a README stating what it does/doesn't do.\n\n**Official packages are optional reference implementations, not a tax** (§14): you may replace, extend, or write custom packages freely, provided nothing bypasses human approval/consent/audit/provider boundary (ADR-018 addendum 4).\n\n---\n\n## 2. `.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`\n\nPath: `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-12-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`\n\nConcrete recipe, in order:\n1. **Orient**: `npm run crm -- app inspect --json`; read `valid` → `problems[]` → `limitations[]` → then `packages[]/capabilities[]/resources[]/actions[]/policies[]/providers[]`. (Note: since Claude Code is running read-only in this pass, this step will need to happen in the actual build pass.)\n2. **Decide it's a package** using the same one-object/one-step/one-rule tests as §PACKAGE_AUTHORING §1.\n3. **Scaffold**: `npm run crm -- package scaffold <name>` / `--apply`; read `modeReason` not exit code; occupied dir refused, invalid name refused with a suggestion.\n4. **Author against contract**: identity fields, `resources`, import only `packages/core/index.js`, evidence records fully `managed`, actions are ordinary action defs, `metadata()` plain + `notModeled`.\n5. **Cross-package deps**: `requires`, opened with your own runtime handles as context (atomicity), smallest interface offered, versioned, never deep-import.\n6. **Register/validate/prove**: the exact five-command ladder (`scaffold → inspect → validate → test → npm test → npm run verify`).\n7. **Claims discipline**: update JTBD matrix conservatively; never describe a stored reference as access/permission/account (directly relevant — this is the same rule delivery's partner engagement follows and your new Partner package should not violate).\n8. **Do not implement**: no registry/npm publish/hot-loading/kernel patch.\n9. Finish with `npm run verify`, run the starter, leave PR open for adversarial review.\n\nAlso states ADR-018 addendum 4's enforcement boundaries explicitly (frozen `domains.get()` summary; capability consumer-name is caller-asserted, not authenticated; nothing here is a sandbox).\n\n---\n\n## 3. `examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/` (full read)\n\n- `README.md` — states this is the **smallest honest package**: one resource, one action, one versioned policy. Models \"a human records how a delivery partner performed on an engagement\" — a rating that \"grants nothing, changes nothing commercially, is never shown to the partner.\" Explicitly says: **\"It attaches to `delivery-partner-engagement`, so it needs the delivery package applied — but it declares no capability requirement, because it only adds an action to a record another package owns.\"** This is a subtlety worth noting for your new package: it works by adding an action *directly onto* `delivery`'s module (`module: 'delivery-partner-engagement'`), which is only legal here because it's an example illustrating a boundary case, not the general rule — the general rule (per §PACKAGE_AUTHORING §4/§Skill point 6) is that acting on another *package's* record needs a declared capability, and `package test` would flag `UNDECLARED_PACKAGE_RECORD_DEPENDENCY` for this exact pattern in a non-example package. Be aware: your new `partners` package should NOT copy this \"act directly on someone else's module without declaring a capability\" trick unless you're deliberately in this same fixture/example role.\n- `modules/partner-scorecard.module.json` — a fully `\"writable\": \"managed\"` manifest (evidence record): `sourceKey` (unique), `engagementId` (unique), `deliveryProjectId`, `partnerRef`, `partnerNameSnapshot`, `score` (integer), `rating` (enum), `reason`, `policy`, `policyVersion`, `policyFingerprint`, `ratedBy`, `ratedAt`.\n- `src/index.js` — full pattern: imports only `{ AppError, ValidationError, definePackage, requiredString }` from `../../../../packages/core/index.js`; exports `PARTNER_SCORECARD_PACKAGE`, `SCORING_POLICY_KIND`, `PARTNER_SCORECARD_RESOURCES`; a versioned policy object (`standardPartnerRatingV1`) with `{name, version, label, config, rate({score, config})}`; `createPartnerScorecardPackage(options)` returning `definePackage({...})`; an action builder `buildRatePartnerAction(config)` requiring `actor.type === 'user'` (else `403 HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`), resolving the policy via `domains.getPolicy(pkg, kind, name, version)`, validating input, then `modules.get(scorecardModule).service.createManaged(...)`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. A simple official composed package: `packages/work`\n\nComposed at `packages/domains/generated/index.js` line: `import { createWorkPackage } from '../../work/src/index.js';` and `createWorkPackage()` in `generatedDomains`.\n\nStructure (`find packages/work`):\n```\npackages/work/README.md\npackages/work/modules/work-activity.module.json\npackages/work/modules/work-task.module.json\npackages/work/src/follow-up.js\npackages/work/src/index.js\npackages/work/src/legacy-tasks.js\n```\n- `README.md` — a good template for what a package README should contain: owns/requires/offers/actions/policies table at top, \"what it is / what it is not\" with an explicit `notModeled` bullet list, enable instructions (module create + compose snippet), explicit lifecycle transition table as prose, capability usage example (`follow-up@1`), idempotency rules, human-actor boundary section, migration-from-legacy section.\n- `src/index.js` — `WORK_PACKAGE`, `WORK_RESOURCES = Object.freeze([TASK_MODULE, ACTIVITY_MODULE])`, `FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY` const, action builders (`buildCompleteTaskAction`, `buildCancelTaskAction`, `buildAddNoteAction`) each with `module`, `name`, `label`, `description`, `actionContract: 1`, `stateField`/`fromStates`/`toState` for lifecycle transitions, `input[]`, `execute({record, input, actor, modules, managed, now})`. A capability creator (`createFollowUpCapability`) returning `Object.freeze({...})` with only bound methods. Final `createWorkPackage(options)` → `definePackage({ packageContract:1, name, version, label, description, resources, requires: [], capabilities, actions, policies: [], metadata() {...huge structured object...} })`.\n- Module manifests use `\"writable\": \"managed\"` throughout for the `work-task` and `work-activity` records (fully evidence-owned), with `enum` state fields (`status`), `index: true` fields for lookups, and free-text fields bounded/control-char-checked in code.\n- **Lifecycle pattern directly reusable for Partner**: `work-task` models an explicit transition table via `stateField: 'status'`, `fromStates`, `toState` on each action, published in `metadata()` as `{states, open, terminal, transitions}`. This is exactly the shape to use for Partner's onboarding→active→suspended→offboarded lifecycle.\n- No dedicated `packages/work/test` directory — tests for a package live under the top-level `/tests` directory (see §9 below), not inside the package directory.\n\n---\n\n## 5. `packages/delivery` — the existing minimal \"partner engagement\"\n\n`grep -rn \"partner\" packages/delivery/src packages/delivery/modules` shows partner touches: `handover.js` (creates the engagement during handover), `handover-policy.js` (validates `{partnerRef, partnerName, reason}`), `economics-actions.js`/`economics.js` (partner cost tracking references `partnerEngagementId`), and the module manifest itself.\n\n**Module**: `packages/delivery/modules/delivery-partner-engagement.module.json` — fully `\"writable\": \"managed\"` record with fields: `sourceKey` (unique), `deliveryProjectId` (unique — **at most one partner per project**), `contractId`, `partnerRef`, `partnerNameSnapshot`, `role` (enum, only value `\"delivery_partner\"`), `status` (enum, only value `\"planned\"`), `workPackageIdsJson`, `workPackageCount`, `scopeSnapshot`, `assignedBy`, `assignmentReason`, `handoverRunId`, `plannedAt`.\n\n**The partner boundary, stated plainly** — `packages/delivery/README.md` lines 147-152 (found via grep):\n> \"A partner engagement is a **business reference and a name snapshot**. It grants no account, no login, no portal, no invitation, no permission, no fee or revenue share and no SLA, and at most one partner is modelled per project. Nothing here notifies the partner or lets them see anything.\"\n\nAlso `packages/delivery/src/index.js:107-119` (metadata) restates: `partner: { model: 'a business reference and a name snapshot...', limitation: 'a partner engagement grants NO access of any kind: no account, no login, no portal, no invitation, no permission, no fee or revenue share, and no SLA. Multiple partners per project are not modelled.' }` and lists `notModeled` including `'partner access', 'partner portal', 'revenue share'`.\n\n**Key facts for interoperability design**:\n- `delivery-partner-engagement` has NO reference field pointing at any \"Partner\" record — it stores only `partnerRef` (a free string) and `partnerNameSnapshot` (a name snapshot taken once, never refreshed). There is currently **no full Partner entity anywhere in the repo** that this snapshot points at.\n- Delivery deliberately models **at most one partner per project** (`deliveryProjectId` is `unique`).\n- If your new `packages/partners` package wants delivery's `delivery-partner-engagement.partnerRef` to *resolve to* a real Partner record, delivery does not currently offer any capability for that, and `delivery-partner-engagement` is owned by `packages/delivery` (a package-owned resource, not a host record) — so any action of yours that reads/writes it would need `delivery` to declare and offer a capability (it currently offers none; `packages/delivery/README.md` capability list only covers `delivery-economics@1`, `delivery-change-management@1`, `delivery-acceptance-evidence@1`). Making the two interoperate (e.g. `partnerRef` optionally resolving to your new Partner record's id) is a **cross-package design decision** you'd need to either (a) leave as two independent partner concepts that share only a business-string convention (`partnerRef`), never joined, or (b) propose as a seam gap / new capability on `delivery` — not something you can just deep-import or reference directly.\n- `packages/delivery/src/execution.js:36,323` similarly disclaims: `'partner access', 'partner portal', 'customer acceptance', 'SLA'` are not modeled.\n- `economics-actions.js` shows `CONTRIBUTOR_TYPES = ['internal', 'partner']` and `PLAN_LINE_KINDS` including `'partner'` — cost-tracking references `partnerEngagementId`, again just the engagement snapshot, not a Partner entity.\n\n**Conclusion**: your new `packages/partners` domain package is architecturally free to define a first-class `partner` (or similarly named) resource with SLA, revenue share, portal-readiness fields, and a real lifecycle — as long as you don't claim `delivery-partner-engagement`'s `resources` entry (that's still owned by `delivery`) and you keep the \"engagement snapshot\" vocabulary distinct from your own entity. There's no existing reference-field wiring between the two today; adding one is new design work, likely a `partnerRef` convention or a documented, deliberately-unenforced business-key correlation, not a DB foreign key (SQLite reference fields per `MODULE_MANIFEST.md` can only reference tables inside the same generated registry and existing \"generated-to-core\" cross references are currently rejected — see §6 below).\n\n---\n\n## 6. `docs/MODULE_MANIFEST.md` and `docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md`\n\n**`docs/MODULE_MANIFEST.md`** — notably, its own worked example is literally a Partner module:\n```json\n// examples/modules/partner.module.json\n{\n  \"manifestVersion\": 1,\n  \"name\": \"partner\",\n  \"description\": \"Channel partners that resell or refer the product.\",\n  \"fields\": [\n    { \"name\": \"name\", \"type\": \"string\", \"required\": true },\n    { \"name\": \"tier\", \"type\": \"enum\", \"values\": [\"silver\", \"gold\", \"platinum\"] },\n    { \"name\": \"territory\", \"type\": \"string\" }\n  ]\n}\n```\nplus a companion reference-field example `examples/modules/partner-contact.module.json` (`partnerId` references `partners`, `required: true`). These are pre-existing files at `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-12-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/examples/modules/partner.module.json` and `partner-contact.module.json` — good sanity check but they are **teaching examples for the manifest CLI**, not registered anywhere/not a package; the module name `partner` (singular, no hyphen) is already used there, so if you build a real `packages/partners` domain you'll likely want a different manifest `name` (e.g. `partner` might collide if these example files are ever applied into the same project, though they live under `examples/modules/` and aren't composed by default — check for collision at build time).\n\nManifest schema essentials: `manifestVersion`, `name` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`), `description`, `table` (optional override), `fields[]` — each with `name` (camelCase), `type` (`string|email|integer|boolean|timestamp|enum|reference`), `required`, `unique`, `values` (enum), `references`/`onDelete` (reference), `writable` (`public`|`managed`), `default`. Auto fields: `id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Money = integer cents + string/enum currency, no composite type. A module whose every field is `managed` is generated read-only (`get`/`list` only, no public create/update) — this is the pattern for evidence records like `partner-scorecard`.\n\n**`docs/MODULE_FACTORY.md`** — the CLI pipeline: `module plan` (read-only) → `module create` (dry-run) → `module create --apply` (writes). For `partner` it generates: `packages/modules/partner/module.manifest.json`, `src/migration.js`, `src/partner-service.js` (CRUD + audit + events), `src/index.js` (module def + `createPartnerModule(deps)`), `tests/partner-module.test.js`, and updates `packages/modules/generated/index.js`. Key semantics: unknown input props ignored; mutation+audit atomic in one SAVEPOINT; events fire only post-commit; managed fields never client-writable, exposed only via `applyManaged`/`ctx.managed`; migrations are immutable once applied (name+SHA256 checksum) — changed SQL under the same name fails loudly. **Important**: for your package, records are created via `npm run crm -- module create packages/partners/modules/<record>.module.json --apply` (the *package's own* modules directory), not via the generic `packages/modules/` factory-owned directory — that's the pattern `partner-scorecard` and `work` both follow (their manifests live under `packages/<pkg>/modules/`, not `packages/modules/`).\n\n---\n\n## 7. `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` — partners/ecosystem/channel + ADR-018\n\n- `PRODUCT.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md`: **no mention** of partners, ecosystem, or channel management anywhere (grep found nothing). `ARCHITECTURE.md` §\"Domain packages\" (lines 63-67) is the relevant general architecture text: packages are optional, self-declared, registered by one static import, validated/fingerprinted/published by the runtime \"without learning the domain's vocabulary.\"\n- `DECISIONS.md` — **no ADR specifically about partners/ecosystem/channel management** exists (grep for \"partner\" in DECISIONS.md returned nothing beyond the header search — meaning the word doesn't appear there at all outside what's in the ADR-018 section itself, which doesn't mention \"partner\"). So there's no prior architectural precedent to reconcile beyond `packages/delivery`'s partner-engagement snapshot.\n- **ADR-018** (`## ADR-018 — The core runtime is a platform; domain behavior belongs in domain packages`, `DECISIONS.md` line 254) and its addenda are the governing decision for package structure:\n  - **The core budget rule**: \"New domain-specific business behavior is not added to `packages/core` unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability\" (≥2 domains need it, or it's one of the named platform responsibilities). This is why your Partner domain must be a package, never touch `packages/core/src`.\n  - Addendum (\"proven by Milestone 12\"): the domain registry seam (`package-registry.js`), `packageContract`, static import registration, fingerprinted policies via `definition_versions` keyed `domain-policy:<domain>:<kind>` — matches what you saw in `partner-scorecard`/`work`.\n  - Addendum 2 (\"what the M12 adversarial review corrected\"): three lessons directly relevant to a Partner domain — (1) **don't claim more than the evidence supports** (e.g., don't call a stored reference \"access\" — echoes the `delivery` partner-engagement disclaimer, and you should apply the identical discipline: a Partner \"portal\" or \"SLA\" field must be real, not aspirational metadata); (2) classification needs orthogonal axes rather than one enum when a thing has two independent dimensions (relevant if Partner lifecycle state and, say, \"tier\"/\"type\" are independent); (3) nothing should read as `active` before an actual business date — use an injectable clock for any future-dated onboarding/offboarding, never a raw `Date.now()`.\n  - Addendum 3 (\"the public domain-package contract\", Milestone 13): this *is* what `PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` documents — `partner-scorecard` was literally built as Milestone 13's worked example of the public contract.\n\n---\n\n## 8. `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` — is there a partner row/template?\n\nPath: `/home/user/agent-crm-panel/runs/TS-12-claude-code-TS-f560c257a824-a1/fixture/docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` (978 lines total; I read lines 1-797).\n\n**No \"partner\" row or domain exists in this matrix.** The only place \"partner\" appears is inside prose describing `delivery` and the `partner-scorecard` custom-package fixture (which the matrix lists as a row: \"Custom-package fixture (`examples/custom-packages/partner-scorecard/`) — `aligned` on the package seam by construction… it exercises only a slice: one resource, one action, no capability of its own\"). There is no template row for a brand-new domain like \"Partner Management.\"\n\n**What is directly usable as a template**: **The Compatibility Backfill Rule** (near line 308-343) — this is the process a new package/capability PR must follow, but note carefully: it applies when your PR introduces or changes a **horizontal** capability (one every existing domain could use). A pure vertical domain package (like your new `partners` package, analogous to `work` or `service`) does **not** by itself require a Compatibility Backfill Rule row *unless* it also introduces something horizontal (e.g. if `partners` were to add a new generic runtime primitive). If it's purely a domain (records, actions, policies), you'd instead add rows the way `Service` was added when it shipped (see the \"Where each domain lives today\" table around line 42-57, and the M15 backfill section around line 414-431 as the precedent for \"a new package that touches nothing else\"): **update the \"Where each domain lives today\" table** with a `partners` row (`Runtime home: packages/partners/`, `Is it a package?: yes`), and if `partners` is package-native from birth like `service`, most of the six-column matrix cells would read `aligned`/`not_applicable` by construction, following the exact reasoning pattern in \"The M15 backfill answer, as the rule requires\" (lines 414-431).\n\nThe five backfill questions to answer in any such PR body (line 320-327):\n```text\nWhich old domains does this touch?\nWhich are already aligned?\nWhich need metadata only?\nWhich need a code backfill?\nWas the Legacy Alignment Matrix updated?\n```\n\n---\n\n## 9. Test organization convention (using `packages/work` as the example)\n\nThere is **no `packages/work/test` directory** — tests for domain packages live centrally under the top-level `/tests` directory (not inside the package folder), named by domain-prefix:\n\n```\ntests/work-operations-e2e.test.js          (595 lines) — full e2e against a real composed app\ntests/work-operations-evidence.test.js     — 500-row display-bound / collection-read evidence\ntests/work-package-absence.test.js         (252 lines) — attached/detached/reattached/absent-from-start proof, each phase in its own child process (Node module caching means in-process re-composition proves nothing)\ntests/work-provenance-and-actor.test.js    (231 lines) — human-actor boundary + provenance\ntests/work-source-key-stability.test.js    (138 lines) — idempotency/business-key retry proof\ntests/work-legacy-task-migration.test.js   — migration-specific\ntests/admin-work.test.js                   — Admin rendering\n```\n\nConventions observed (from `work-package-absence.test.js` and `work-operations-e2e.test.js`):\n- Node's built-in `node:test` + `node:assert/strict`.\n- e2e tests spin up real composed apps via `createAccordoApp` and drive actions through `app.runAction({module, action, recordId, input, actor})`.\n- Absence/detach tests use `spawnSync` to run each phase (attached/detached/reattached/absent) in a **separate child process** against a temp-copied project tree, rewriting `packages/domains/generated/index.js` between phases, because Node caches modules by URL in-process.\n- Shared test helpers live in `tests/helpers/` — notably `tests/helpers/package-conformance.js` (the shared package-conformance assertions cited in `PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md` §12) and `tests/helpers/contracts-project.js` (a reusable project/boot helper with composition toggles like `withDelivery`, `withService`, `withLifecycle`, `withWork`, `followUp`, used across many e2e suites including `tests/work-operations-e2e.test.js`).\n- General package-conformance tests: `tests/package-contract.test.js` (436 lines), `tests/package-test-command.test.js` (602 lines, the DX4 harness tests), `tests/custom-package-e2e.test.js` (144 lines, tests `partner-scorecard` specifically and fingerprints kernel files before/after to prove no kernel change was needed).\n\nFor a new `packages/partners` package you'd want, following this exact naming convention: `tests/partners-operations-e2e.test.js`, `tests/partners-package-absence.test.js`, plus whatever lifecycle-transition and idempotency tests are relevant, all under the top-level `/tests` directory — not inside {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Mcww5spwJs2UWTJEaosD7b","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t// @ts-check\n2\t\n3\timport { AppError, definePackage } from '../../core/index.js';\n4\timport {\n5\t  ACTIVITY_KINDS,\n6\t  ACTIVITY_MODULE,\n7\t  TASK_MODULE,\n8\t  TASK_OPEN,\n9\t  TASK_STATES,\n10\t  TASK_TERMINAL,\n11\t  TASK_TRANSITIONS,\n12\t  bounds,\n13\t  closingKey,\n14\t  createFollowUp,\n15\t  optionalSafeText,\n16\t  recordActivity,\n17\t  requireHumanActor,\n18\t  resolveModule,\n19\t  safeText,\n20\t  sortTimeline,\n21\t} from './follow-up.js';\n22\t\n23\t/**\n24\t * **Work v1 — tasks and activity evidence.**\n25\t *\n26\t * Two records and no more: a **Task** — a unit of human follow-up work arising\n27\t * from a business event — and an **Activity** — an append-only, user-facing\n28\t * entry on a subject's timeline. One capability, `follow-up@1`, lets a\n29\t * consuming domain action create a deterministic follow-up inside its own\n30\t * transaction.\n31\t *\n32\t * **Named `work` deliberately.** `tasks` describes half of it and `activities`\n33\t * the other half; `engagement` would promise a channel that does not exist; and\n34\t * `work-management` would promise planning, scheduling and capacity — which\n35\t * would also collide with Delivery's shipped work packages and milestones.\n36\t * `work` is the smallest noun covering both records, and it is bounded below\n37\t * rather than by its name.\n38\t *\n39\t * **What it deliberately is not, and each is published in `metadata()`:** a\n40\t * scheduler, reminders, calendar sync, email/SMS/WhatsApp, notifications,\n41\t * authenticated or secure assignment, RBAC, a project-management suite, a\n42\t * universal event bus, or a replacement for audit and trace.\n43\t *\n44\t * **Audit versus Activity.** Audit and trace stay the technical record of every\n45\t * write; Activity is the curated semantic one, with a closed four-entry\n46\t * vocabulary. Nothing here projects one into the other: the event bus dispatches\n47\t * after commit (ADR-012), so a projection would either escape the originating\n48\t * transaction or need Jobs/Outbox, which does not exist. Every Activity row is\n49\t * written inside the transaction of the action that caused it.\n50\t */\n51\t\n52\texport const WORK_PACKAGE = 'work';\n53\texport const WORK_RESOURCES = Object.freeze([TASK_MODULE, ACTIVITY_MODULE]);\n54\texport const FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY = Object.freeze({ package: WORK_PACKAGE, capability: 'follow-up', version: 1 });\n55\t\n56\texport {\n57\t  ACTIVITY_KINDS, ACTIVITY_MODULE, TASK_MODULE, TASK_OPEN, TASK_STATES,\n58\t  TASK_TERMINAL, TASK_TRANSITIONS, createFollowUp, sortTimeline,\n59\t};\n60\t\n61\t/** @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames] */\n62\tfunction resolvedNames(moduleNames = {}) {\n63\t  return {\n64\t    task: moduleNames[TASK_MODULE] ?? TASK_MODULE,\n65\t    activity: moduleNames[ACTIVITY_MODULE] ?? ACTIVITY_MODULE,\n66\t  };\n67\t}\n68\t\n69\t/** @param {any} modules @param {string} name */\n70\tfunction activityService(modules, name) {\n71\t  // Through resolveModule: an unregistered name throws out of the registry, so\n72\t  // reading it optionally would let a bare 404 replace this named refusal.\n73\t  const service = resolveModule(modules, name)?.service;\n74\t  if (!service?.createManaged) {\n75\t    throw new AppError(`The work package is installed without its \"${name}\" records`, {\n76\t      code: 'WORK_STORAGE_INVALID', status: 500,\n77\t    });\n78\t  }\n79\t  return service;\n80\t}\n81\t\n82\t/** The subject envelope as it was recorded on the task; never re-resolved. */\n83\tfunction subjectOf(record) {\n84\t  return { resource: record.subjectResource, id: record.subjectId };\n85\t}\n86\t\n87\t/**\n88\t * `complete` — the only way a task becomes `completed`.\n89\t *\n90\t * `fromStates: ['open']` is the transition table, enforced by the action\n91\t * runtime before this code runs: a completed or cancelled task answers `409\n92\t * INVALID_STATE` naming the state it is in. There is no reopen, so both\n93\t * terminal states are genuinely terminal.\n94\t *\n95\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n96\t */\n97\texport function buildCompleteTaskAction(moduleNames) {\n98\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n99\t  return {\n100\t    module: names.task,\n101\t    name: 'complete',\n102\t    label: 'Complete task',\n103\t    description: 'Record that this follow-up work is done. A human decision; it notifies nobody and schedules nothing.',\n104\t    actionContract: 1,\n105\t    stateField: 'status',\n106\t    fromStates: [TASK_OPEN],\n107\t    toState: 'completed',\n108\t    input: [{ name: 'note', type: 'string', required: false, hint: 'Optional: what was done. Recorded on the timeline as text.' }],\n109\t    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, managed, now }) {\n110\t      const completedBy = requireHumanActor(actor, 'Completing a task');\n111\t      const note = optionalSafeText(input.note, 'note', bounds.reason);\n112\t      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n113\t      const completedAt = now();\n114\t      const task = await managed(record.id, {\n115\t        status: 'completed',\n116\t        completedBy,\n117\t        completedAt,\n118\t        closingReason: note,\n119\t      });\n120\t      // Same transaction as the transition: a completed task without its\n121\t      // timeline entry is a pair this package never produces.\n122\t      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n123\t        sourceKey: closingKey(record.id, 'completed'),\n124\t        kind: 'task_completed',\n125\t        subject: subjectOf(record),\n126\t        taskId: record.id,\n127\t        body: note,\n128\t        occurredAt: completedAt,\n129\t        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'complete' },\n130\t      });\n131\t      return { task, activity };\n132\t    },\n133\t  };\n134\t}\n135\t\n136\t/**\n137\t * `cancel` — work that will not be done, with the reason on the record.\n138\t *\n139\t * Cancellation is not completion and the two are never collapsed: a cancelled\n140\t * task is evidence that somebody decided the work was not needed, and reading\n141\t * it as \"done\" would be wrong in the only direction that matters.\n142\t *\n143\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n144\t */\n145\texport function buildCancelTaskAction(moduleNames) {\n146\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n147\t  return {\n148\t    module: names.task,\n149\t    name: 'cancel',\n150\t    label: 'Cancel task',\n151\t    description: 'Record that this follow-up work will not be done, with a reason. A human decision; nothing is notified.',\n152\t    actionContract: 1,\n153\t    confirm: true,\n154\t    stateField: 'status',\n155\t    fromStates: [TASK_OPEN],\n156\t    toState: 'cancelled',\n157\t    input: [{ name: 'reason', type: 'string', required: true, hint: 'Why the work is not being done. Required.' }],\n158\t    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, managed, now }) {\n159\t      const cancelledBy = requireHumanActor(actor, 'Cancelling a task');\n160\t      const reason = safeText(input.reason, 'reason', bounds.reason);\n161\t      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n162\t      const cancelledAt = now();\n163\t      const task = await managed(record.id, {\n164\t        status: 'cancelled',\n165\t        cancelledBy,\n166\t        cancelledAt,\n167\t        closingReason: reason,\n168\t      });\n169\t      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n170\t        sourceKey: closingKey(record.id, 'cancelled'),\n171\t        kind: 'task_cancelled',\n172\t        subject: subjectOf(record),\n173\t        taskId: record.id,\n174\t        body: reason,\n175\t        occurredAt: cancelledAt,\n176\t        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'cancel' },\n177\t      });\n178\t      return { task, activity };\n179\t    },\n180\t  };\n181\t}\n182\t\n183\t/**\n184\t * `add-note` — the one place a human writes free text into the timeline.\n185\t *\n186\t * It has **no `fromStates`**: a note on a closed task is legitimate — often it\n187\t * is the explanation of why it closed the way it did — and refusing it would\n188\t * make the timeline stop exactly where it becomes interesting.\n189\t *\n190\t * It carries **no source key**, deliberately: two identical notes are two\n191\t * statements, not a duplicate, so there is nothing to deduplicate against. That\n192\t * makes a retried note request a second note, which is stated as\n193\t * `MANUAL_NOTES_ARE_NOT_IDEMPOTENT` rather than hidden behind a key that would\n194\t * silently swallow the second one.\n195\t *\n196\t * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n197\t */\n198\texport function buildAddNoteAction(moduleNames) {\n199\t  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n200\t  return {\n201\t    module: names.task,\n202\t    name: 'add-note',\n203\t    label: 'Add note',\n204\t    description: 'Append a human note to this task\\'s subject timeline. One capability, `follow-up@1`, lets a\n * consuming domain action create a deterministic follow-up inside its own\n * transaction.\n *\n * **Named `work` deliberately.** `tasks` describes half of it and `activities`\n * the other half; `engagement` would promise a channel that does not exist; and\n * `work-management` would promise planning, scheduling and capacity — which\n * would also collide with Delivery's shipped work packages and milestones.\n * `work` is the smallest noun covering both records, and it is bounded below\n * rather than by its name.\n *\n * **What it deliberately is not, and each is published in `metadata()`:** a\n * scheduler, reminders, calendar sync, email/SMS/WhatsApp, notifications,\n * authenticated or secure assignment, RBAC, a project-management suite, a\n * universal event bus, or a replacement for audit and trace.\n *\n * **Audit versus Activity.** Audit and trace stay the technical record of every\n * write; Activity is the curated semantic one, with a closed four-entry\n * vocabulary. Nothing here projects one into the other: the event bus dispatches\n * after commit (ADR-012), so a projection would either escape the originating\n * transaction or need Jobs/Outbox, which does not exist. Every Activity row is\n * written inside the transaction of the action that caused it.\n */\n\nexport const WORK_PACKAGE = 'work';\nexport const WORK_RESOURCES = Object.freeze([TASK_MODULE, ACTIVITY_MODULE]);\nexport const FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY = Object.freeze({ package: WORK_PACKAGE, capability: 'follow-up', version: 1 });\n\nexport {\n  ACTIVITY_KINDS, ACTIVITY_MODULE, TASK_MODULE, TASK_OPEN, TASK_STATES,\n  TASK_TERMINAL, TASK_TRANSITIONS, createFollowUp, sortTimeline,\n};\n\n/** @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames] */\nfunction resolvedNames(moduleNames = {}) {\n  return {\n    task: moduleNames[TASK_MODULE] ?? TASK_MODULE,\n    activity: moduleNames[ACTIVITY_MODULE] ?? ACTIVITY_MODULE,\n  };\n}\n\n/** @param {any} modules @param {string} name */\nfunction activityService(modules, name) {\n  // Through resolveModule: an unregistered name throws out of the registry, so\n  // reading it optionally would let a bare 404 replace this named refusal.\n  const service = resolveModule(modules, name)?.service;\n  if (!service?.createManaged) {\n    throw new AppError(`The work package is installed without its \"${name}\" records`, {\n      code: 'WORK_STORAGE_INVALID', status: 500,\n    });\n  }\n  return service;\n}\n\n/** The subject envelope as it was recorded on the task; never re-resolved. */\nfunction subjectOf(record) {\n  return { resource: record.subjectResource, id: record.subjectId };\n}\n\n/**\n * `complete` — the only way a task becomes `completed`.\n *\n * `fromStates: ['open']` is the transition table, enforced by the action\n * runtime before this code runs: a completed or cancelled task answers `409\n * INVALID_STATE` naming the state it is in. There is no reopen, so both\n * terminal states are genuinely terminal.\n *\n * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n */\nexport function buildCompleteTaskAction(moduleNames) {\n  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n  return {\n    module: names.task,\n    name: 'complete',\n    label: 'Complete task',\n    description: 'Record that this follow-up work is done. A human decision; it notifies nobody and schedules nothing.',\n    actionContract: 1,\n    stateField: 'status',\n    fromStates: [TASK_OPEN],\n    toState: 'completed',\n    input: [{ name: 'note', type: 'string', required: false, hint: 'Optional: what was done. Recorded on the timeline as text.' }],\n    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, managed, now }) {\n      const completedBy = requireHumanActor(actor, 'Completing a task');\n      const note = optionalSafeText(input.note, 'note', bounds.reason);\n      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n      const completedAt = now();\n      const task = await managed(record.id, {\n        status: 'completed',\n        completedBy,\n        completedAt,\n        closingReason: note,\n      });\n      // Same transaction as the transition: a completed task without its\n      // timeline entry is a pair this package never produces.\n      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n        sourceKey: closingKey(record.id, 'completed'),\n        kind: 'task_completed',\n        subject: subjectOf(record),\n        taskId: record.id,\n        body: note,\n        occurredAt: completedAt,\n        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'complete' },\n      });\n      return { task, activity };\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * `cancel` — work that will not be done, with the reason on the record.\n *\n * Cancellation is not completion and the two are never collapsed: a cancelled\n * task is evidence that somebody decided the work was not needed, and reading\n * it as \"done\" would be wrong in the only direction that matters.\n *\n * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n */\nexport function buildCancelTaskAction(moduleNames) {\n  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n  return {\n    module: names.task,\n    name: 'cancel',\n    label: 'Cancel task',\n    description: 'Record that this follow-up work will not be done, with a reason. A human decision; nothing is notified.',\n    actionContract: 1,\n    confirm: true,\n    stateField: 'status',\n    fromStates: [TASK_OPEN],\n    toState: 'cancelled',\n    input: [{ name: 'reason', type: 'string', required: true, hint: 'Why the work is not being done. Required.' }],\n    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, managed, now }) {\n      const cancelledBy = requireHumanActor(actor, 'Cancelling a task');\n      const reason = safeText(input.reason, 'reason', bounds.reason);\n      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n      const cancelledAt = now();\n      const task = await managed(record.id, {\n        status: 'cancelled',\n        cancelledBy,\n        cancelledAt,\n        closingReason: reason,\n      });\n      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n        sourceKey: closingKey(record.id, 'cancelled'),\n        kind: 'task_cancelled',\n        subject: subjectOf(record),\n        taskId: record.id,\n        body: reason,\n        occurredAt: cancelledAt,\n        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'cancel' },\n      });\n      return { task, activity };\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * `add-note` — the one place a human writes free text into the timeline.\n *\n * It has **no `fromStates`**: a note on a closed task is legitimate — often it\n * is the explanation of why it closed the way it did — and refusing it would\n * make the timeline stop exactly where it becomes interesting.\n *\n * It carries **no source key**, deliberately: two identical notes are two\n * statements, not a duplicate, so there is nothing to deduplicate against. That\n * makes a retried note request a second note, which is stated as\n * `MANUAL_NOTES_ARE_NOT_IDEMPOTENT` rather than hidden behind a key that would\n * silently swallow the second one.\n *\n * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n */\nexport function buildAddNoteAction(moduleNames) {\n  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n  return {\n    module: names.task,\n    name: 'add-note',\n    label: 'Add note',\n    description: 'Append a human note to this task\\'s subject timeline. Safe text only; it is not sent anywhere.',\n    actionContract: 1,\n    input: [{ name: 'body', type: 'string', required: true, hint: 'The note. Plain text, at most 1000 characters.' }],\n    async execute({ record, input, actor, modules, now }) {\n      requireHumanActor(actor, 'Adding a note');\n      const body = safeText(input.body, 'body', bounds.noteBody);\n      const activities = activityService(modules, names.activity);\n      const activity = await recordActivity({ activities, actor }, {\n        sourceKey: null,\n        kind: 'note',\n        subject: subjectOf(record),\n        taskId: record.id,\n        body,\n        occurredAt: now(),\n        source: { package: WORK_PACKAGE, action: 'add-note' },\n      });\n      return { activity };\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * `follow-up@1` — the declared capability, and the only automatic write path.\n *\n * It is created with the **caller's** runtime handles, so everything it writes\n * happens inside the caller's transaction. It returns a frozen object with two\n * methods and hands out no service, no table, no query handle and no mutable\n * state — there is nothing here a consumer could use to reach around the\n * contract.\n *\n * @param {Record<string, string>} [moduleNames]\n */\nexport function createFollowUpCapability(moduleNames) {\n  const names = resolvedNames(moduleNames);\n  return {\n    name: FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY.capability,\n    version: FOLLOW_UP_CAPABILITY.version,\n    description:\n      'Create exactly one deterministic follow-up task and its creation activity inside the caller\\'s transaction, keyed by a business identity the caller supplies.',\n    /**\n     * @param {{modules: any, actor?: unknown, now?: () => string, consumer?: string}} context\n     *   `consumer` is added by `PackageRegistry.capability()` from the identity\n     *   it resolved and verified against that package's own declared\n     *   `requires`. A consumer that wants to know \"did this already happen?\"\n         * asks this rather than listing.\n         * @param {string} sourceKey\n         */\n        findBySourceKey(sourceKey) {\n          const service = resolveModule(context.modules, names.task)?.service;\n          if (!service?.listWhere) {\n            throw new AppError(`The work package is installed without its \"${names.task}\" records`, {\n              code: 'WORK_STORAGE_INVALID', status: 500,\n            });\n          }\n          return service.listWhere({ sourceKey: String(sourceKey) })[0] ?? null;\n        },\n      });\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/** @param {{modules?: Record<string, string>}} [options] */\nexport function createWorkPackage(options = {}) {\n  return definePackage({\n    packageContract: 1,\n    name: WORK_PACKAGE,\n    version: 1,\n    label: 'Work items and activity evidence',\n    description:\n      'Owns follow-up tasks and an append-only user-facing activity timeline, and offers one capability so a domain action can create a deterministic follow-up inside its own transaction. It schedules nothing, reminds nobody and assigns to nobody.',\n    resources: [...WORK_RESOURCES],\n    // Nothing. Work is consumed, never a consumer, so no composition that adds\n    // it can create a dependency cycle.\n    requires: [],\n    capabilities: [createFollowUpCapability(options.modules)],\n    actions: [\n      buildCompleteTaskAction(options.modules),\n      buildCancelTaskAction(options.modules),\n      buildAddNoteAction(options.modules),\n    ],\n    // None. A versioned policy earns its fingerprint when two consumers share a\n    // rule; these two share none — one wants a lead's name and a due date, the\n    // other a contract intent and no date. See ADR-030.\n    policies: [],\n    metadata() {\n      return {\n        workContract: 1,\n        task: {\n          states: [...TASK_STATES],\n          open: TASK_OPEN,\n          terminal: [...TASK_TERMINAL],\n          transitions: { open: [...TASK_TRANSITIONS.open], completed: [], cancelled: [] },\n          reopen:\n            'not supported in v1. A repeated business round is a new task under a new source key; un-completing work would need a second lifecycle nothing here has',\n          dueAt:\n            'evidence only. Nothing schedules on it, nothing fires from it, and no clock changes a status because of it. A read-only due/overdue may be computed at an injected instant and never writes. It must still name a day that existed: an impossible date is refused rather than rolled over to the next real one (ADR-028)',\n          assignment:\n            'there is no assignee field. This package does not model assignment, ownership or routing, and nothing here is secure assignment',\n        },\n        activity: {\n          kinds: [...ACTIVITY_KINDS],\n          appendOnly: 'no action in this package updates or deletes an activity, and the module has no public write path',\n          order:\n            'a timeline is read oldest-first by occurredAt, compared by code point and never by host locale collation, so the order does not depend on the machine, the ICU build or LANG. TIMELINE_ORDER_TIES_ARE_ARBITRARY — entries recorded at the same instant have no business order and are broken by created stamp then id, purely so two readers see the same list',\n          versusAudit:\n            'audit and trace stay the technical record of every write; this is the curated user-facing one with a closed vocabulary. Nothing projects one into the other, and there is no asynchronous projection engine',\n          content:\n            'the only free text is a note body, bounded and control-character-free. No provider payload, attachment, email body or chat message is stored',\n        },\n        subject: {\n          shape: ['subjectResource', 'subjectId', 'subjectOwner', 'subjectOwnerPackage', 'subjectLabel'],\n          owner: ['host', 'package'],\n          provenance: ['sourcePackage', 'sourceAction'],\n          referentialIntegrity:\n            'SQLite cannot enforce a foreign key whose target table varies per row, and this package does not pretend it can. Verified rather than assumed: a call that cannot prove an open transaction on that connection is refused 500 WORK_TRANSACTION_REQUIRED before the first write, so no half pair can be produced by calling it from a script or outside an action',\n          hostLimitation:\n            'HOST_ACTIONS_CANNOT_DECLARE_CAPABILITIES — PackageRegistry.capability() resolves consumer against registered packages, so a host application action cannot open this capability. The `follow-up@1` capability closes over it for\n * consuming *packages*; the host application's own actions import it directly,\n * because `PackageRegistry.capability()` resolves its `consumer` against\n * registered packages and the host is not one (see `docs/plans/\n * activity-task-operations.md` §9). Two callers, one function, no fallback and\n * no second code path that could drift.\n *\n * It writes through the caller's own `modules` handle, so both rows land inside\n * the caller's transaction. If the caller's transaction rolls back, the Task and\n * its Activity roll back with it — there is no separate connection, no queue and\n * no post-commit hook anywhere in this package.\n */\n\nexport const TASK_MODULE = 'work-task';\nexport const ACTIVITY_MODULE = 'work-activity';\n\nexport const TASK_STATES = Object.freeze(['open', 'completed', 'cancelled']);\nexport const TASK_OPEN = 'open';\nexport const TASK_TERMINAL = Object.freeze(['completed', 'cancelled']);\n\n/**\n * The whole transition table. Not a rank, not a comparison, not an ordering:\n * a map from a state to the states it may become, and every state that is not\n * listed as a key can become nothing.\n *\n * `open -> completed` is direct and deliberate. An `in_progress` state would\n * change no read, no action and no refusal in this milestone, so it would be a\n * label that also forces a rule nobody asked for. There is **no reopen**:\n * a repeated business round is a new task under a new source key, which the\n * idempotency rule already supports, and un-completing work would need a second\n * lifecycle nothing here has.\n */\nexport const TASK_TRANSITIONS = Object.freeze({\n  open: Object.freeze(['completed', 'cancelled']),\n  completed: Object.freeze([]),\n  cancelled: Object.freeze([]),\n});\n\n/** The closed Activity vocabulary. Four entries, and adding a fifth is a decision. */\nexport const ACTIVITY_KINDS = Object.freeze(['task_created', 'task_completed', 'task_cancelled', 'note']);\n\nconst MAX_SOURCE_KEY = 200;\nconst MAX_TITLE = 200;\nconst MAX_LABEL = 200;\nconst MAX_BODY = 1000;\nconst MAX_REASON = 300;\nconst MAX_ID = 200;\n\nconst CONTROL_RE = /[\\u0000-\\u001f\\u007f-\\u009f\\u2028\\u2029]/;\nconst RESOURCE_RE = /^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$/;\nconst PACKAGE_RE = /^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$/;\nconst SOURCE_KEY_RE = /^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9:._@/-]*$/;\n\n/**\n * **Why there is no clock heuristic here, and where the guarantee lives instead.**\n *\n * A key that moves identifies nothing, and M16a shipped exactly that defect\n * once: a deterministic key that carried `now()` made every retry a new\n * business fact. The first cut of this package answered it with a regex that\n * refused any ISO instant and any run of 13 or more digits. That regex is\n * **gone**, because it was a one-domain bug fix promoted into a universal false\n * restriction, and it refused real business identities:\n *\n *   - `customer:1234567890123` — a 13-digit external customer number;\n *   - `stripe-evt:1712345678901` — a provider event id;\n *   - `order:9876543210987654` — a 16-digit order number;\n *   - `meeting:2027-01-31T14:00` — a business event whose *scheduled* instant is\n *     deliberately part of its identity and is stable under every retry.\n *\n * None of those moves between two calls, which is the only property that\n * matters, and none is distinguishable from a clock by looking at the string.\n * Work is a **generic** capability: it cannot infer a caller's business\n * identity, so it does not pretend to. It refuses what it can actually judge —\n * structurally invalid syntax and unbounded length — and the stability of the\n * identity stays with the caller who owns it, proven by that caller's own retry\n * test. Every consumer in this repository has one, in\n * `tests/work-source-key-stability.test.js`, and every key is derived from a\n * committed record id rather than from a clock.\n */\n\n/** @param {unknown} value @param {string} field @param {number} max */\nfunction safeText(value, field, max) {\n  if (typeof value !== 'string') throw new ValidationError(`${field} is required`, { field });\n  const trimmed = value.trim();\n  if (trimmed === '') throw new ValidationError(`${field} is required`, { field });\n  if (trimmed.length > max) throw new ValidationError(`${field} must be at most ${max} characters`, { field });\n  if (CONTROL_RE.test(trimmed)) {\n    throw new ValidationError(`${field} must not contain control characters or line breaks`, { field });\n  }\n  return trimmed;\n}\n\n/** @param {unknown} value @param {string} field @param {number} max */\nfunction optionalSafeText(value, field, max) {\n  if (value === undefined || value === null || value === '') return null;\n  return safeText(value, field, max);\n}\n\n/**\n * The framework's actor authority, and **only** that.\n *\n * This used to be a local re-implementation that also did\n * `id.trim().slice(0, 200)`. Truncation is not a bound, it is a **merge**: two\n * distinct identities that share their first 200 characters became one string,\n * so `openedById`, `completedBy` and every Activity `actorId` would name a\n * person who may not have done it — while the audit row written by the same\n * transaction, through the kernel's own `normalizeActor`, still carried the\n * full id. Two records of one write disagreeing about who did it is the worst\n * kind of evidence defect, because both look authoritative.\n *","numLines":120,"startLine":1,"totalLines":628}}}
